ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-23-CD211123.u1	Good morning, ladies and gentlemen. The meeting was resumed. Sections 12, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, European Union and Cooperation, and 34, Financial relations with the European Union We continue the debate on the opinion of the draft Law on General State Budgets for the year 2022. First of all, the debate on sections 12 and 34 has the floor on behalf of the Government, the Minister for Foreign Affairs, the European Union and Cooperation. [[Ministry of Foreign Affairs, European Union and Cooperation]] [[Financial relations with the European Union]]
ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-23-CD211123.u2	Ladies and gentlemen, I come before you today to set out the general outlines of the budgets of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, the European Union and Cooperation. These are budgets that serve Spain's foreign policy and its foreign action, which is a State policy because it has the defence of the Spaniards and the projection of their values and interests as the highest priority. Foreign policy, European policy and cooperation policy are and can only be State policies, always keeping in mind the best interests of Spain and the Spanish and the projection of their values. External action is central to the lives of the Spaniards, because our currency, the vaccines we put on, the gas we consume, all of which we manage to work together with our partners, allies and friends in the world. For this reason, my department's consolidated budget of 2022 has been significantly increased, amounting in total to EUR 1928.65 million, with a total increase of 4.13% over the previous year, reflecting both the outlook for the future and the solidarity of our society, as reflected in our foreign policy and our foreign action. We look to the future to address the major challenges we face: support for multilateralism, the profound change in our economic and social model, through the digital transition and the green transition in the face of the climate emergency, as well as assistance to Spanish citizens who are abroad; they are always at the heart of our foreign policy. The NATO summit next year 2022, the Spanish Presidency of the European Union in the second half of 2023 and the plans for the digitisation of our consular services are very concrete elements of this vision for the future, as are solidarity budgets that accompany a fair economic recovery, with an increase in the development cooperation funds of the Cervantes Institute and the subsecretariat, in the latter case focused on the execution of European funds and the digitisation of the ministry. These objectives are met by the reforms in which the Ministry is working within the framework of the European Plan for Recovery, Transformation and Resilience and for whose implementation a budget of 48.5 million euros is foreseen in 2022. I would like to stress that these budgets have a direct impact on the lives of the Spaniards. Firstly, because they defend our interests in the international sphere and, secondly, because they give priority to the needs of Spanish citizens and companies abroad. Our future as a country, and therefore the future of all Spaniards, is inextricably linked to the future of our neighbours, our partners and global recovery. For us to go well inside, we have to do well outside, and we have to work well and together with our partners, friends and allies in the world. Under this premise, the budgets that I present today have the gaze placed on the future of our country as a member of the European Union and as an engine member of the European Union; as a country and as an engine of the Ibero-American community of nations, as a leading multilateral actor and also as a major donor to the international community. The policy that has the greatest impact on the Spaniards is undoubtedly European policy. Europe is no longer a dimension of our external action, but a reality integrated into the Spanish identity itself. Being Spanish is being European, but being European also means sharing institutions, currency, vaccine and now, for the first time, common debt. We cannot do all that alone, we work with our European partners. In this context, Spain will assume the Spanish Presidency of the Council of the Union, which will take place during the second half of 2023. Its preparation is a key element of our external action, as are the European funds approved by the European Transformation, Recovery and Resilience Plan, which have highlighted the centrality of Spain's membership of the European Union in our external action. For this reason, the ministry which I lead, within its Secretariat of State for the European Union, is increasing by EUR 4.80 million as a result of the allocation necessary to meet the first preparatory expenses of the Presidency, to which a total of EUR 26.21 million will be allocated, and thus will be carried out a capacity increase, both in the permanent representation of the European Union in Brussels and in the central services, which will allow the Presidency to be strategically designed, promoting initiatives that represent us as Europeans and as Spaniards. This Presidency and our role in the European Union are hand in hand with citizens with their participation and direct involvement in mechanisms such as the Conference on the Future of Europe, in collaboration with the Autonomous Communities and the FEMP and also by increasing the allocation of the Ministry's programme We Talk about Europe. We also look to the future, within our own structures and organisation, in a way that is consistent with the objectives we have set ourselves. The 48 million euros of the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan will be used to make access to information more effective by citizens and organisations linked to external action. The Cervantes Institute will increase its budget by 8.48%, receiving 26.3 million euros of its total allocation of 161 million euros to boost the teaching of Spanish abroad. The budgets of the ministry I run meet a clear objective: to accompany recovery, fair, sustainable, and supportive recovery. This ministry has played an essential role in the recovery of the pandemic abroad. The commitment to donate vaccines already amounts to 50 million doses by the end of the first quarter of 2022. Just yesterday, together with the President of the Government, we held an event about the donation of vaccines, which received the congratulations of the Director-General of WHO. But if there is one element that marks this desire to accompany the recovery and to reflect the solidarity of our society in the budgets, it is official development aid. This official development aid has an increase of 15.39% over 2021, including a 28% increase in the budget of the Spanish Agency for International Development Cooperation, the best instrument, the best tool we have for international solidarity. This figure is one of the fundamental pillars of the increase in official aid for global development, and will reach the levels of a decade ago, with an amount of €3506.59 million. This represents an increase of 93.4% compared to 2015, the year in which ODA was the lowest recorded in general government budgets, and also represents an increase of EUR 391.62 million over the year 2021, from 0.26% to 0.28% of gross national income. Years of decline and loss of Official Development Assistance are reversed. This Government believes that cooperation should be a State policy, which can only be a State policy, and that is why we are seeking to provide cooperation with new regulatory frameworks to modernize the system and to accompany economic efforts to achieve its objectives. Spain also focuses on the global effort, on multilateralism, as the main means of achieving our objectives. To this end, the ministry has a budget of €433.40 million, which is divided into compulsory contributions, which are key to ensuring our country's commitments, and into other voluntary ones; compulsory ones reach €388.8 million and voluntary ones, worth €39.18 million, will allow us to finance international programs or actions that correspond at every specific moment to our priorities. By way of example, this is the mechanism that allowed us to quickly allocate EUR 4 million to humanitarian aid projects in Afghanistan at the beginning of the crisis. We will continue to contribute to alleviating the impact of this crisis on the Afghan population through our voluntary contributions to agencies working in the field. We are also committed to contributing to peace through peacekeeping operations, to which we have allocated a budget of EUR 165.37 million. These contributions represent a strong reflection of our commitment to peace, Spain's commitment to peace, and of our willingness to remain part of this goal in the main theatres of conflict: in Lebanon, Congo, Colombia, Mali, for referring only to the most relevant. The budgets of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, the European Union and Cooperation are geared as a whole, but also in very specific headings, to the defence and protection of our compatriots abroad. Ladies and gentlemen, there are currently 2.84 million Spaniards living abroad; that would be equivalent to the third province of Spain by population number, by number of residents. Each of these Spaniards brings diversity and wealth to Spain, and they are ambassadors of our values, our principles and our culture. Nor can we obviate our companies, whose internationalization and presence in many parts of the world, increasingly in more and more parts of the world, clearly contributes to Spain's progress. That is why I wanted the Directorate-General for Economic Diplomacy to become directly dependent on the Minister; it is an expression of the commitment to his needs and to the promotion of his internationalization. Therefore, our consular action must also adapt to the new times that millions of compatriots live in different regions of the world, often in very difficult and complex circumstances. To protect and assist your interests, we have developed one of the most extensive, most secure and most efficient consular networks. For this we have launched, also with the funds of the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan, a Consular Digital Transformation Plan, with a total of 110 million euros, of which 21.6 million correspond to the budget of 2022. With this plan we are committed to modernizing consular information systems and technological infrastructures to give better service to Spaniards abroad. The ministry I run is very much in mind for our fellow countrymen who face difficulties in distant places. In order to assist them, the Ministry has a budget appropriation of EUR 3.37 million, of which EUR 2.3 million is earmarked for support for the protection of Spanish nationals abroad. With this amount, aid is granted to both welfare institutions and subsistence economic aid, and special economic aid is also granted to Spaniards deprived of their liberty in foreign prisons and for repatriations. One of my priorities is to give a new orientation to our country's foreign service, more suited to the needs of our citizens in a very changing world, as well as to global challenges. The 5907 members of the family of the foreign service -1475 working in the central services in Spain and 4432 working abroad - deserve a ministry that is equal to their daily work in the service of the Spaniards. To meet these needs, the Ministry's administration and general services area has a budget of $495.53 million, of which €79 million is for the maintenance of common services and €416 million is for embassies and consulates. This item will cover the needs arising from the transfer of the ministry to its headquarters in the Plaza del Marqués de Salamanca, Madrid, after almost fourteen years of absence from that headquarters. In conclusion, ladies and gentlemen, we are presenting an expansive draft budget to meet the priorities we have set ourselves as a ministry and as a government. It fulfils the objective of providing us with an effective and modern external service that serves to convey what can only be a State policy and, above all, responds to the solidarity vocation of Spanish society within, but also outside our borders, and to the commitment of this Government to work for a just and sustainable recovery also in the global context. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]
ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-23-CD211123.u3	Thank you very much, Minister. [[Pause.-A cleaning service worker proceeds to disinfect the speaker's rostrum]] In defence of amendments, Mrs Martínez Granados has the floor for the Citizens' Parliamentary Group.
ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-23-CD211123.u4	Thank you, President. Ladies and gentlemen, first of all, I would like to thank the minister for his assistance in the debate on this point, because it seems that, surprisingly, there is a minister in this government who is interested in the budget debate. So, thanks for coming. We have tabled amendments, both to the joint text and to the high and low ones within this title, to reflect the priorities and criteria of the European regulation of the Next Generation Funds. In this way, it aims to guide more clearly and with more certainty the criteria to be met by investments with European funds, mainly through the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism. These amendments establish, for example, that investments with European funds are aimed at reforms contained in the recommendations of the European Semester for Spain, which are compatible with national energy and climate plans, fair transition plans and the rest of the plans on the use of European Union funds; that they are projects that are not in third country investment plans or that may affect public order or security, that do not finance current expenditure and respect the principle of additionality; that they generate added value at European level, i.e. that the value of the contribution of European funds would have been impossible to obtain by the Member State alone; that they are compatible with the European Union's Gender Equality Strategy 2020-2025; that at least 37% of the funds are intended for mitigation and adaptation to climate change. We introduce all this so that it is respected within the General Budgets of the State. In addition, investments are required to include a memo on how this expenditure will contribute to the objectives set. In this way, it aims to ensure that the Government details how the investments it plans to commit with European funds contribute to the objectives set at European Union level. In this way, we will facilitate Spain's accountability to the Council of the European Union on the sound administration of these funds. Finally, these amendments also sought to oblige the Government to report and appear before the Joint Commission of the European Union whenever there were any changes in investments made with European funds that were approved within the General State Budgets; that is, to strengthen the accountability mechanism to prevent changes in European funds or investments on the sole basis of the Government. This amendment was vetoed and could not be debated or voted on. We have tabled high- and low-level amendments to items that included or were affected by the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism; amendments that met all the requirements of the European Union, but we have found that we have also been vetoed. And why - Minister, I asked him at the appearance the other day - can the government itself present items in the budgets that the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism does use, affecting current expenses, and the rest of the parties - meeting the criteria that the European Union marks - are vetoed the amendments aimed at resolving or modifying those items? We don't understand, minister, we can't understand. From the Citizens' Parliamentary Group we have also been proposing that at least 15% of official development assistance be devoted to humanitarian action. The motivation is that there is an increasing need for such emergency response actions in developing countries, due to the impact of climate change and the increased incidence of natural disasters that also cause humanitarian crises resulting from armed conflict. In fact, on the proposal of our group, the subcommittee for the study of the regulatory framework for Spanish cooperation agreed to set that objective in its conclusions, which was subsequently ratified by the Plenary of the Congress of Deputies, and despite this the Government has not fulfilled this mandate. It was therefore proposed in this amendment to reflect this commitment again in the State's general budgets, but we have also been denied this amendment. We have tabled, as I said, many other amendments which we believe are important for the exercise of all our external actions. For example, the programme of professional practices for young people Margarita Salvatierra. It is proposed to create a programme of paid professional practices for young people in the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, European Union and Cooperation and in the Spanish Agency for International Development Cooperation, both in its central services in Madrid and in its offices abroad. This encourages the inclusion of young talent in these institutions, while ensuring adequate remuneration for young people who contribute their talent to the administration. The duration would be between twelve and twenty-four months to finance it, and it is proposed to allocate an amount of EUR 850,000 in order to carry out this programme of practices. We have also tabled amendments to ensure that there is a promotion of Spaniards in the institutions of the European Union. EUR 250,000 will be allocated to the Secretariat of State for the European Union to finance promotional activities in the European Commission. We have also tabled amendments to promote the Hispanic legacy. Spain does not devote sufficient resources and efforts to combat black lies and legends about its historical presence in various parts of the world, especially in the Americas. That is why we have witnessed the destruction of cultural elements linked to the Hispanic legacy. Hence our amendment with that specific heading for the Cervantes Institute to carry out defence work in the promotion of Spanish and Hispanic heritage. Finally, we also tabled an amendment to strengthen cultural, scientific and educational relations between Spain and the United States. The Fulbright Commission is doing a commendable job, financing stays, scholarships and training of teachers, researchers, Spanish students in the United States and Americans visiting Spain. Taking into account the importance of good transatlantic relations and the need to strengthen our country's knowledge of the United States and the existing links, we proposed an amendment with an additional EUR 250 000 from Spain's contribution to the Fulbright Commission for the financing of more such scholarships. I hope that some of them that remain alive can be supported by the other parliamentary groups. Thank you so much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-23-CD211123.u10	Thank you, President. Thank you, Minister. The truth is that it would be foolish not to acknowledge that this budget debate comes in a much more uncertain and complex international context than we expected a few months ago. On the one hand, it is clear that from the government and from the progressive and left forces accompanying him in this House great budgetary efforts have been made to try to give this crisis a different way out from the one that occurred in 2008; here is the example of the ERTE, here is the example of the different social shields, without which thousands of jobs, businesses, life, would surely have been wrecked without contemplation. On the other hand, however, it is true that the social emergency and the climate emergency have not remitted and are adding an important part of the population to a state of anxiety and general concern. It seems to me that the key is to know where this situation comes from and how this emergency is generated. The right-wing response we see here every day: to use catastrophe and alarmism to generate more fear, to create scapegoats that face the people below and to hide the responsibility that in all this has the huge concentration of private power that has occurred at this time, it seems to me that with an obvious objective, that is to create a state of paralysis or a sort of state of shock in the sense of Naomi Klein to re-end us the adjustment policies and cuts that were implemented after the 2008 crisis, and that have led us to an absolute disaster as a society. Day and day we also hear many neoliberal landowners tell us that the problem of inflation or the absence of certain supplies is due to public debt, at the level of wages, pensions, etc. But the truth is that this is only sustained if we do not pay attention to two key elements of the neoliberal economy, such as financialization and job insecurity. How is there not going to be instability in the prices of energy, transport, certain foods if for decades an indecent concentration of power has been favored in the energy and food market! How is there not going to be instability if for decades financial markets have been allowed to speculate with soil, with rivers, with nature, as if they were one more commodity! How will there be no shortage at this time of certain products if the working conditions in the international supply chains, in the maquilas, in the global subcontracts are absolutely shameful! Therefore, what we see here is a structural problem that we do not want to see, and it is the existence of an open and senile capitalism with signs of ever more visible exhaustion. What is the planetary eruption of the far right but the attempt to respond to the decadence of this zombie capitalism, generating fear and generating violence? Against who? We saw it a week ago, when VOX voted against two ILO conventions, against the working classes, against women, against migrants, against those who protest against this systemic violence. That sort of preventive counter-revolution that we are experiencing is the privileged 1% programme of the world at this time, but, as our colleague Pablo Bustinduy said recently, to stop it is not enough to invoke the fear of those who generate fear and hatred, there are fundamental structural responses, both at the domestic and at the European and international level, because, let us not deceive ourselves, ladies and gentlemen, metal workers in Cadiz, Alicante, the canned ones in Vizcaya, the supermarkets in Castilla and León will not cease to strike because they are stigmatised, because they are intimidated by tanks or because they are promised a green future that you may find them dead, they will only do so if their rights are respected and the employers fulfil their obligations, and if social and environmentally just reindustrialisation ceases to be a mere sign of becoming a tangible reality, also for the working people of southern Europe. ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-23-CD211123.u14	Thank you, President. Minister, ladies and gentlemen, good morning. With the non-financial spending budgets of these general state budgets for 2022, the Spaniards will reach a figure of 422 billion euros, expenditure never reached in Spain and which amounts to 5.5 billion euros more than for 2021, something that does not seem reasonable given the circumstances of debt, which already exceeds 1.4 times our gross domestic product, or, what is the same, each of us, each of us, each Spanish owes 30,000 euros; once again expanding and unbelieving budgets, based on unrealistic and false assumptions, with an overestimation of revenues and an underestimation of expenditures, being difficult to compare them with those of previous years due to the constant reorganizations of the ministry, a clear demonstration of their lack of political direction. Entering into detail, we find matches that are unclear neither in their ends nor in their destiny. I give a couple of examples, of the many I could cite: for studies and technical work, the budget for 2022 is almost 50 million euros for the entire ministry, 7 million more than in 2021, a huge amount, given the high qualification of the officials and employees of the ministry who could perform them in many cases; on the other hand, they are jobs that on most occasions are only excuses to pay favors to benefactors of the ideological agenda. We are not against them, on the contrary, but we must be demanding with them. Sometimes globalist plans have very real negative consequences for the Spanish on foot, who always see their interests second to those. The budget continues to maintain extraordinary contributions to these organizations, which have not served their objective of placing members of the Government in any of these institutions, with slightly more than EUR 500 million this year, which, if we add them to last year's ones, are already worth one billion. As for the new and emerging Secretariat of State for Ibero-America and the Caribbean and Spanish in the World, its budget of 80 million euros is actually reduced to some 4.5 million, because 76 million are transfers to the Cervantes Institute, which should be responsible for defending Spanish outside our borders. The creation of this new Secretariat of State would be good news if it really brought about some real change in Spain's foreign policy, but we are afraid that it will be just a new office of placement for some of its multiple clients, nothing disposable, because, analyzing in more detail those 4.5 million, it is not explained the employment of 2.6 million euros in personnel only to manage 460,000 euros and transfer 76 million euros to another institution. Thus, empty of content and real management, it is also possible to think that this Secretariat of State is a negotiator in charge of the turbulent affairs of this coalition government on the other side of the Atlantic, namely, the Delcy case, the financing of Podemos by Cuba and Venezuela, the payments to Mr. Monedero, the rescue of Plus Ultra, the attempt to escape Bolivian leaders through our embassy there, the payments of the Embassy of Ecuador to the environment of Podemos, and so we could continue for a while. As for the Cervantes Institute, its current fit within this new Secretariat of State does not mean anything real, just moving organs from the Secretariat of State of Cooperation and changing names. The Cervantes Institute will have slightly more than 161 million euros for next year, against 173 million last year. However, this budget is misleading, as it will receive transfers of €76 million from its Secretariat of State, as well as an investment of €26 million in various digitisation programmes associated with the European Union Recovery and Resilience Mechanism, and to all of the above will have to be added revenue for the provision of services of €46 million. With regard to the Secretariat for International Cooperation, here is the great ambush of the budget. It accounts for more than half of foreign spending, which means prioritising third-country nationals in the face of caring for Spanish interests and caring for Spanish citizens abroad. Moreover, it is a huge economic volume in the hands of a highly ideological agency, Aecid, with recent financial scandals millionaires, disappearance of millions in Panama, and very low level of real control of its impact and effectiveness. By 2022 this Secretariat of State will have 673 million, which, in addition to the 491 budgeted for Aecide, make a total of just over 1164 million euros, 168 million more than for this year and 313 if we take the year 2020 as a reference, and all this without counting the nearly 300 million for vaccines of the 143.A program, which have placed as part of the Ministry of Health. We do not have here the rest of the items of other ministries that are counted as development aid and that, according to their own data, would reach 3152 million euros by 2022, 0.28% of the gross national income. And here there is an important risk of great magnitude, as government parties have been advocating increased development aid cooperation up to 0.7 per cent of GDP. This would be a way to consolidate a trapping growth by taking advantage of the exceptional nature of vaccine donations in the 2022 budget for the future to allocate that volume to their chiringuitos and ideological subsidies. What are you going to tell the Spaniards about this? And the inhabitants of La Palma, in particular? Do you think it is normal to increase external spending in this way, when the situation of economic, social and now also energy crises is out of control? Or are you going to tell them that a thousand or three billion is nothing, kiddo? [[Applauds]] With regard to financial relations with the European Union, it is noted that with EUR 26 million for diplomatic action before the European Union, this Secretariat of State is the poor relative of the Ministry, despite which we again denounce its unjustifiable duplicity with the General Secretariat of the European Union, with a rank of undersecretary and subordinate to the former. Thus, with the current situation in Spain, this increase in spending in the ministry does not seem highly recommended or feasible unless the Government continues to breach its promises, as it usually does, and continues to raise indirect direct taxes indiscriminately and indebt the State to limits that we had never before known, more than 1.4 times the gross domestic product, and one more abuse: the appointment by royal decree of 19 October 2021 of an ambassador to the Special Mission for a Feminist Foreign Policy who is neither a diplomat nor a career official. Finally - I conclude, President - when the resources of the nation are scarce and the prospects for the future are not very flattering, the most sensible and advisable seems to be the concentration of efforts and the containment of expenditure...

[[Pause.-A cleaning service worker proceeds to disinfect the speaker's rostrum]] We continue with the debate in section 28, Ministry of Science and Innovation. For the section presentation, the Minister of Science and Innovation has the floor on behalf of the Government.
ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-23-CD211123.u22	Thank you, President. Good morning, ladies and gentlemen. Today I appear before you to detail the budgets for scientific research and innovation in Spain for the year 2022. These are the budgets of the drive to science and innovation to transform the country. Investing in science is investing in development, it's investing in the future. As with the VOCID pandemic and the volcanic eruption of La Palma, in the face of the dangerous threat of climate change, in the face of the grey tsunami of population ageing or in the face of the imperative digital green transformation, it will not be enough to cross your fingers or look elsewhere. The answers are in science, and for it to move forward we must provide it with resources and activate courageous scientific policies. Society knows that and we in this room can't miss it. A large majority of the Spanish population believe that the Government should invest more in science and technology, according to all demoscopic studies. In the face of the transformations that our country has to undergo, we need more than ever our scientific, innovative and entrepreneurial talent, our most valuable capital. With this firm conviction, the Government of Spain plans to devote the largest investment in science and innovation in our country's history. With a total budget of €3843 million, our ministry almost doubles its resources compared to 2020; 90% is increased in two years. Ladies and gentlemen, these are also the budgets of the pact. With them we are on the path marked by the objectives of the Pact for Science and Innovation, an agreement between the social, economic and political actors to achieve a public science system in line with the excellence of our innovative scientific community, and a consensus to consolidate a stable and lasting investment in science and innovation that reaches the average investment of the European Union in R & D+i, that is the challenge. And they are also the budgets of science by and for people. We will create more and better opportunities so that people who research and innovate can generate the knowledge and solutions that improve the life of the citizenry and contribute to the modernization of our productive fabric and to a solid, sustainable, cohesive and just growth that reaches all the houses and territories of Spain. We will advance these objectives through the budget that I will present to you, with which we will achieve five goals: more Spanish research, better scientific infrastructures, more culture of innovation in companies, more science and innovation in all the territories of Spain and, consequently, more and better opportunities for those working in research, technology and innovation in our country. I'll explain it to you one by one. First goal: more research. We want to continue to generate Spanish science and for this we will fund more research projects through competitive competition calls. After ten years of cuts and stagnation, we will increase the opportunities to carry out research in Spain. In this context, it should be noted that the State Research Agency, our country's main scientific funding instrument, increases its budget by 112% in 2022 compared to 2020, and will therefore have 1358 million euros in 2022 to finance research. With this historical budget, we continue to bet on basic science, traditionally supported by the State R & D & i Plan and strengthen research focused on strategic projects of the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan, such as those related to the ecological and digital transition. And we will also strengthen health research through the Carlos III Institute of Health, our flagship health research vessel that this year will launch calls worth 278 million euros, both for basic research and for projects applied to advanced therapies and precision medicine. Second goal: To improve the scientific capabilities of our research centres. If we want higher quality research, we will also have to invest in equipping the public science system with more capabilities, infrastructure and equipment of excellence, modernising with this shock plan and which will last for years in the system. To this end, we will invest a record budget of 1818 million euros to strengthen our public research bodies, which are the Carlos III Institute of Health, the more than 150 centers of the Higher Council for Scientific Research and the Center for Energy, Environmental and Technological Research. This represents a 61% increase in resources for our IPOs compared to those available in 2020. These investments will enable the creation of international reference centres, thanks to the implementation of new infrastructures such as the first laboratory of maximum biological safety in Spain, a new oceanographic ship or laboratories of experimentation in advanced batteries. Third goal: more innovation culture. If there is to be more innovation in our country, we must promote a greater culture of innovation and strengthen collaboration between the public and private sectors. That is why, from our innovation agency, the CDTI, we will devote EUR 1566 million in grants and credits to promote business R & D+i, 48% more than in 2020. It is an unprecedented public investment to attract private investment, key to the recovery and transformation of our country and to achieve together, the public and private sectors, the desired target of 2% of GDP in R & D by 2023. We will also advance in the public-private collaboration of the 21st century, with a new concept of an entrepreneurial state, which has materialized in new calls of the Misiones and Cervera programs, as well as with pre-commercial public procurement and public innovation procurement so that innovative companies have opportunities to develop advanced prototypes aligned with the needs of our society, having in the public administration its first user. Fourth goal: more science and innovation in all territories. We will continue to promote that science and innovation will have opportunities in all autonomous communities, strengthening territorial cohesion and deepening the territorial expansion of our R & D & i. The Ministry of Science and Innovation is already present in all the territories of Spain, and we will continue to strengthen this relationship with the investment of 136.8 million euros in scientific and technical infrastructures distributed throughout the Spanish territory. In addition, and in a complementary way, for the first time, the Government of Spain and the autonomous communities will jointly investigate, through the complementary plans, a pioneering network of co-governance and financing in R & D+i. These are eight programmes around eight strategic areas: marine science, biodiversity, quantum communication, biotechnology applied to health, energy and renewable hydrogen, new materials, agro-food and astrophysics and high-energy physics. Each of these programmes will involve the State and at least four autonomous communities, and will mobilize 456 million euros until 2025. We will also accompany the municipalities, so that they can fulfill their roadmaps through innovation. We launched a new programme to support the incorporation of innovation agents into municipalities, through the Impulse Network, which will have an investment of 10 million euros in 2021 and 2022. Finally, I explain our main goal: to create more and better opportunities for our talent. As a result of all the above, more jobs will be created in our country in the R & D & I sector. The first to do so is the public sector. The Government's two budgets have given research staff priority consideration for public employment, with a replacement rate of 120 per cent. This means that for every ten researchers who retire, twelve are incorporated. This measure makes it possible to rejuvenate the staff and reverse the effects of the cuts applied by the People's Party Government, which led to a brain drain and the blocking of the professional careers of an entire generation of young researchers; only one thing: since Pedro Sánchez is President of this Government, 2746 new positions of investigating officer have been approved. In addition, this year and next we will greatly increase the supply of contracts for doctors in the public sector. In 2022 we will dedicate 151 million euros to increase the salary and the number of contracts, mainly of the programs of excellence Juan de la Cierva and Ramón and Cajal. These increases will involve the recruitment of more than 2500 doctors between 2021 and 2023. In the field of innovative talent in companies, in 2022 we will continue to open opportunities, prioritizing the hiring of doctors in technology-based companies within the calls Neotec, Cervera and Torres Quevedo, endowed with 90 million euros. This budget that I have just presented to you will be accompanied by the deployment of structural reforms of the system that will fight against the precariousness, inequality and administrative obstacles that persist in the R & D+i system. This Government is aware of the problems and is determined to reverse this situation through the reform of the Law on Science, Technology and Innovation, which will come to this House in early 2022, as well as other normative instruments that will come to recognize the uniqueness of the Spanish scientific and innovative system. President, ladies and gentlemen, I shall conclude. Through the budget that I have just detailed we seek a transformative science and innovation, with greater social and economic impact. Ladies and gentlemen, we are facing a new opportunity to create a more resilient and knowledge-driven tomorrow and an innovative look. We play our future and that of the next generations. We are also faced with the opportunity to transform the Spanish scientific and innovative system to make it possible. I therefore trust your support for these budgets, which will help boost science and innovation by and for people as a lever of progress and social well-being in Spain. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-23-CD211123.u36	Thank you very much, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, we are faced with historic budgets that seek a fair recovery; budgets that bravely face the challenges that have challenged us for decades: social cohesion, territorial cohesion, digital transition, equality and ecological transition. And, logically, the justice system could not be left out of this great country challenge. I appear before the plenary of the House to report on the figures provided for in the section for the Ministry of Justice in the draft Law on General Budgets of the State for the year 2022. In the area of justice, we intend with these budgets to take a step forward in transforming our administration of justice into a medullary public service within the framework of the welfare state. We have for this purpose, duly coordinated, with the own funds and also with the funds that come from the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan, which support the implementation of three of the reforms in terms of efficiency that are in the preliminary draft phase at this time: the preliminary draft of the law of procedural efficiency, the preliminary draft of the law of organizational efficiency and the preliminary draft of the law of digital efficiency. The preliminary draft of the Law on Procedural Efficiency of the Public Justice Service introduces procedural reforms to give more agility to the processing of judicial proceedings and to enhance the means of settling disputes appropriate and parallel to the jurisdiction itself. The preliminary draft Organic Law on Organizational Efficiency of the Public Service of Justice is committed to the creation and establishment of courts of instance, the evolution of the peace courts towards the new municipal judicial offices and also the establishment of the Judicial Office adapted to this new judicial organization, which has been being sued for many years by the justice sector. Finally, the preliminary draft of the law on the digital efficiency of the public service of justice will introduce in the Administration of Justice the new technologies with which our society is already living, increasingly digitized, because the judicial system cannot be looking inwards, but must be looking outwards, centered on citizenship and also centered on the rest of the administrations. Ladies and gentlemen, the budget resources of the Justice portfolio in this draft budget amount to EUR 2284 million, EUR 235.4 million more than the previous ones, representing a total increase of 11.5 %. Of these 2284 million, 2208 correspond to the participation of the Ministry of Justice, which represents an increase of 232 million euros - i.e. 11.8 % - while the participation of the General Council of the Judiciary is 76 million euros, which implies an increase of 4,2 %. Within the overall amount provided for the political objectives of the Ministry of Justice, EUR 210 million is collected as an annuality planned for 2022 under the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan, an increase of EUR 182 million and a variation of 656,2 %. Allow me, ladies and gentlemen, to stop for a few minutes in the Recovery Plan and in its importance for improving the capacities of the justice system as an element of country transformation for its contribution to the 2030 Justice Plan, which is perfectly aligned with the axes and objectives of the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan. Justice 2030 is not a plan of the Ministry, but it is a plan to co-rule with the autonomous communities - with those with transferred competences in the field of justice, twelve of them - and also governed at institutional level with the General Council of the Judiciary and with the Attorney General's Office, but also with the Spanish Federation of Municipalities and Provinces and with the rest of the professionals in the field of justice. In a breakdown of the expenditure chapters, the main budget items in section 13 of the Ministry of Justice would be as follows. Chapter 1, staff costs, amount to EUR 1646.74 million. This is the amount that covers the salaries of staff working in the Ministry of Justice, which represents 73.3% of the Ministry's budget. For the financial year 2021, it will increase its appropriations by EUR 42.30 million, from EUR 1604.44 million to EUR 1646.74 million, by 2.6%. It is true, however, that we need to make improvements in staffing within the framework of the three preliminary drafts I have pointed out earlier, which support and which are the scaffolding of Justice 2030, so that, once they come into force after the parliamentary procedure, we will be faced with a new organisational model. In this direction, the public employment offer of 2021 has been an ambitious step. Recently, 150 public employees have been incorporated into the Ministry of Justice and some 700 new entrants have been requested in the coming years. Among the improvements that we are going to undertake is a 120 per cent replacement rate for the Legal Service of the Administration of Justice and the rest of the officials in the service of the Administration of Justice; the creation of 70 new judicial bodies; the expansion of the tax base in 70 places, as well as the transformation of 219 third-level positions into second-level positions in the Ministry of Public Prosecutions, which has been being requested by the Prosecutor's Office for many years, and a job offer of up to 200 places for entry into judicial and fiscal careers, with a view to the numeric stabilization of the offer of public employment for judges, judges and prosecutors, the reduction of the interinity and coverage of the new procedural and structural organizational needs of justice in the medium and long term. In Chapter 2, current expenditure on goods and services, there is an increase of EUR 5.66 million, representing an increase of 4%, from EUR 141.16 million to EUR 146.82 million. Chapter 3, financial expenditure, is increased by EUR 305,000, to EUR 330,770, due to changes in the budgetary allocation of certain items. With regard to current transfers, Chapter 4 increases by EUR 18.5 million. This is an increase of 27.8%, from EUR 65.03 million to EUR 83.08 million. In this chapter, in the first place, we can highlight EUR 15 million from the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan for free legal assistance in mediation - covering both that of the autonomous communities with transferred competences and the territory of the ministry - as well as the maintenance of the endowment intended to finance the psychological assistance of the victims, provided by psychologists and psychologists who are integrated in the offices of assistance to victims and other professionals within the territory of the ministry. Finally, but not least, there is also the increase in the allocation for free legal aid from EUR 49.18 million to EUR 53.29 million, an increase of 8.4%. We include here both specialized assistance resulting from the commitments of the State Pact against Gender Violence, with a 40% increase, and specialized assistance for the provision of the implementation of Organic Law 8/2021, on comprehensive protection for children and adolescents from violence. As regards Chapter 6, real investments, it is increasing by EUR 46.25 million compared to the budget for 2021, by 23.7%, reaching EUR 241.2 million in total. Of these, 14,5 % is allocated to infrastructure, equipment and buildings, while the remaining 85,5 % will be devoted to the transformation of justice. One of the keys to this chapter is the improvement and consolidation of technological means. EUR 206.61 million is dedicated to investments in new computer equipment and also in programming licences. The other great key of this chapter is that of works and equipment of buildings of judicial buildings, with 26.42 million for the year 2022, of which 72.37 % - i.e. 19.12 million - correspond to new work and 27.63 % -7.30 million - are destined for replacement works. The largest increase in all budget items takes place in capital transfers, Chapter 7, which receive a contribution of EUR 120.71 million, from the current EUR 8.01 million to EUR 128.72 million. Of these, 125.83 million will be allocated to the autonomous communities through the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan for co-governance projects. Chapter 8, financial assets, maintains the same allocations as in 2021, a total of EUR 619 910. Ladies and gentlemen, in relation to the autonomous bodies under the Ministry of Justice, the General Judicial Mutuality increases its budget for 2022 by 12%, up to EUR 125.04 million. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to refer to the Centre for Legal Studies, which will have a budget of EUR 16.25 million, an increase of EUR 3.5 million and 27.4% more than the budget approved for 2021. Within the appropriations for the Centre for Legal Studies, I would like to conclude by highlighting a measure which represents a great step forward, a huge step forward: a credit of EUR 1 620 000 to meet the scholarship programme for the preparation of oppositions to judicial and fiscal careers, to the higher body of lawyers and lawyers of the Administration of Justice and to the body of lawyers and lawyers of the State, in order to promote real and effective equality in access through the elimination of barriers of a socio-economic nature, democratizing access to these professional careers so that people with a great vocation to devote themselves to the justice sector can do so without having to face these barriers. We are faced with a vertebrating project of society that will allow us to correct one of the main weaknesses of the current system of access to these bodies of excellence. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, reducing the digital divide, digital cohesion, social cohesion, territorial cohesion, dialogue between administrations, dialogue between the various political forces, shaping justice as a core public service of the welfare state, of the social and democratic rule of law that characterizes us, and increasing equal opportunities in access to the public service and also in access to justice for all our citizens. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

Section 14, Ministry of Defence We continue with the debate in Section 14, For the section presentation, I have the floor on behalf of the Government, the Minister of Defence. [[Ministry of Defence]] [[Pause.-A cleaning service worker proceeds to disinfect the speaker's rostrum]]
ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-23-CD211123.u58	Thank you, President. Thank you, ladies and gentlemen. I should like to explain the content of the draft State General Budgets Act for the year 2022 with regard to section 14, which corresponds to the Ministry of Defence. I would like to begin by paying a well-deserved tribute to the members of the Armed Forces, who daily perform generous, discreet, silent and very effective work, which we must highlight in this House; a work resulting from a very high level of capacity, preparedness and availability, which makes credible deterrence against threats to our spaces of sovereignty and our interests, both in national and international settings. At the same time, capacities to deal with emergency situations and crises of an unpredictable nature are made available to society. It has been the case of operations and missions carried out to combat COVID-19, to alleviate the effects of the Filomena storm, to quell large fires, the successful evacuation process of Spanish citizens and collaborators from Afghanistan or the work that is now being done in connection with the eruption of the La Palma volcano. From here we want to send all our affection and support to the citizens of the island. In all of them, the Armed Forces have adapted their capabilities to the needs of each emergency, and have maintained their action as long as necessary. This has been possible thanks to the generous effort of the men and women of the Armed Forces, a work recognized and valued by Spanish society. Ladies and gentlemen, the budgets in Section 14 I am presenting are realistic and in line with the current budgetary framework, integrating the appropriations necessary to meet the requirements arising from the objectives set out for defence policies in the National Defence Directive and the 2020 Defence Policy Directive. Budgets that, first, guarantee the operational capacity of the Armed Forces; second, continue the process of modernizing the capabilities of the Armed Forces and, third, ensure the fulfilment of Spain's obligations as a responsible nation, solidarity and commitment to international security and peace. These are budgets that continue to place their focus on the men and women of the Armed Forces, consolidating the pay improvement made this year. The budget for Section 14 amounts to EUR 10 155.3 million, an increase of 7.9%, and EUR 743 million over 2021. It is an increase that is also consolidated in the autonomous bodies under the Ministry of Defence, such as the National Institute of Aerospace Technique, INTA, and the National Intelligence Centre, which has increased its resources by 7.5 per cent. Focusing already on the main chapters of the defence budget, it should be noted that the appropriations allocated to Chapter 1 amount to EUR 5061.8 million, representing 49.81% of the department's total budget; these appropriations are increased by EUR 133.7 million, 2.71% compared to the 2021 budget. This, together with the increase in the replacement rate from 110 to 120 %, will allow for the continuation of the personnel planning and civilian reinforcement policy of the Ministry of Defence for the years 2020-2022. Similarly and as we have said, the forecasts in chapter 1 will make it possible to maintain the momentum of the process of adjusting the salaries of military personnel initiated in the previous year. In the field of education, training, specialization and career guidance, the department maintains its objective of meeting professional expectations with the Comprehensive Professional Development Strategy. To this end, resources increase by EUR 8.4 million, an additional 2.9% compared to 2021. In this context, the basis has been laid for the digital transformation of military education, promoting the Gnoss Project, within the Government's Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan, which will combine training paths with communication and work orientation programmes. To this end, ladies and gentlemen, there are EUR 3 million budgeted for the financial year 2020-2022. We also continue to work on improving the living conditions of military personnel, especially troops and seafarers, by increasing resources for social action projects, support for the study of children, for persons with disabilities and for the professional promotion of military personnel, as well as the establishment of children's education centres, and the Armed Forces Social Institute, which is constantly adapting to protect and maintain their proximity to members. Also - and this is important - the departure of hospital care in the Armed Forces is increased by 8.6 million euros, 5.7% more than in 2012. Military health resources are improved, implementing lessons learned in the current crisis to ensure support for society. Ladies and gentlemen, the appropriations for Chapter 2, current expenditure, amount to 909 million, representing 8.9% of the Ministry's total budget and an increase of 13.3 million. Provision is made for the services required for the routine activities of our military at bases and quarters, as well as for the supplies, communications services, cleaning and security of military personnel. Current expenditure related to environmental and energy sustainability, as well as defence diplomacy activities and those aimed at strengthening defence culture are included. This chapter 2 initially includes EUR 100 million of the provision for peacekeeping operations, which will provide a more balanced response to the current financial requirements of our units abroad. In addition, EUR 164 million will be added, which, through the planned enlargements, will allow for the completion of funding for these activities. Ladies and gentlemen, the appropriations in Chapter 4, current transfers, amount to EUR 455.5 million, representing 4.5% of the ministry's total budget, with an increase of EUR 25.6 million over 2021. They are intended to cover the financial contributions to the international security and defence agencies to which Spain is a party, as well as the operating costs of INTA and the National Intelligence Centre, as well as part of the costs of teaching and training our staff. Ladies and gentlemen, with the resources in Chapter 6, dedicated to real investment, the objective is to obtain and develop the defence capabilities necessary to confront the current strategic context and to obtain a joint, viable, balanced and sustainable force. The available appropriations amount to EUR 3647.3 million and represent 36% of the Ministry's total budget; this represents an 18% increase over the year 2021. The internal allocation of the economic envelopes of this chapter is carried out following the defence planning process, which has inspired and underpinned the considerable investment effort that has been made in recent years, after many years in which these investments had not been made. These investments include the special modernization programmes, with a budget of 2848 million, an increase that will enable us to meet the financial commitments made in previous years and the progress of new projects. These investments, in addition to providing the necessary capabilities for our Armed Forces to carry out their missions and which, in addition, are carried out with certainty, are - and I am very concerned about this - a key instrument for Spain's economic and social impulse and recovery, as they generate important economic and technological returns in the industrial fabric due to their technical and dual nature. They contribute to the growth of national GDP and are a driver of employment that will help maintain and create a significant number of jobs. I mean, like, the S80 submarine in Cartagena or the F 110 frigates in the Ferrol region. The provision for the modernization of the remaining systems and equipment, as well as logistical support for the maintenance of all systems, equipment and infrastructure, includes €573.4 million this year, an increase of €60.4 million over 2021. These items will be increased by the provision for peacekeeping operations, which provide for EUR 210 million in the initial budget, to be added to the provisions of the proposed credit extensions during the financial year. We are aware of the need to continue with the investment effort in sustenance, which drags, as I said before, a structural deficit due to the cuts suffered in previous times. I would like, ladies and gentlemen, to highlight the effort in research, development and innovation, which is fundamental to achieving the technological advantage, which in 2022 represents an investment of EUR 30.5 million. In this field we are integrated into the various European initiatives stemming from the European Defence Fund and from the Permanent Structured Cooperation, known as the Pesco. The budget of the Ministry of Defence includes appropriations from the European Union Recovery Funds, with a total of EUR 50 million earmarked to promote projects for the necessary digital transformation affecting information and communication systems, training and cybersecurity mechanisms. Ladies and gentlemen, these budgets represent a major effort and consolidate growth that aims to provide the necessary budgetary stability in order to continue to meet the objectives of defence policy in all its axes. In addition, they serve to support the action of the Armed Forces not only in purely military missions, but also in collaboration with other agencies, enhancing social policies, job creation, innovation, technological development and the projection of Spain in the world. In short, these budgets enable Spain to continue to have leadership in defence policies, both within the European Union and within NATO, feeling proud of the perception that in these two areas of the European Union and NATO have of security and defence policies. We are well aware that we must continue along the same lines begun this year, improving the salaries of the men and women of the Armed Forces. A major effort has been made this year, and that is the will we want to continue to work with. At the same time, we want to support these modernization projects because they make us have a better equipped and better prepared armed forces, but which, as I said before, are an industrial attraction for many regions that live precisely on defence industry projects. A clearly Spanish defence industry. A Spanish defence industry that we want to have projection abroad, with projects shared with Germany or France, as is the new generation plane. I'm done, ladies and gentlemen. I thank the parliamentary groups for supporting this budget, stressing once again that investing in security and defence is investing in rights, freedoms, modernity, technology and job creation. That commitment by a modern, effective armed forces, with men and women like the ones we have in them, committed, prepared and serious, is the best way to ensure a fully democratic country, with freedom, democracy and rights, but at the same time, with obligations to ensure peace and security in the world, as they do at present three thousand men and women of the contingents of the Armed Forces who are in places as far away as Lebanon, Mali or the Central African Republic, to whom they have taken advantage to pay my tribute and my testimony, as do the more than two hundred military personnel of the EMU, Earth Army and the Navy who are at present on Palma Island. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-23-CD211123.u72	Thank you very much, Mr President. Good afternoon, ladies and gentlemen. Indeed, I appear on this occasion to defend sections 10, 15 and 38 of the general budgets and, as the President said, to address centralised recruitment, the headings for the Ministry of Finance and Public Service and, finally, the system of territorial financing. Ladies and gentlemen, first of all, I would like to stress the need for this country to have public accounts for the coming year to consolidate economic recovery, so that it can reach all people, all sectors and all territories. This task is essential for many reasons. Firstly, by offering stability and confidence to the country, to Spanish society, which has gone so badly and we are still ending this pandemic and the corresponding recovery of an economic crisis that it had associated. Secondly, because the budget opens up a door to hope and a door to the future, especially for young people, so that they can find solutions to their main problems. Thirdly, because it guarantees security and certainty for the elderly, with revalued pensions and more resources than ever before for care for dependency and care. Ladies and gentlemen, of course, the budget has as its priority the revitalization of the welfare state, the public services and the safety net that guarantee rights and allow us to be equal in the social contract that binds us all. That, ladies and gentlemen, is all the more reason for us, regardless of the way in which amendments are being carried out throughout this process, to have a high-level view and, therefore, to be aware of the responsibility we have so that next year these public accounts can help and contribute to the progress of all administrations. By entering fully into the subject of debate, these sections focus on three fundamental objectives: the first is to promote the modernisation of public administrations, strengthening social legitimacy and advancing the efficiency of public policies, but also in transparency and accountability. Linked to this point, secondly, we want to take advantage of the opportunity of the funds of the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism to enhance the role of the public sector as a driving force for the dynamism of both employment and the economy, just by setting an example from administrations in the digital and ecological transition, but also in progress on gender equality. Thirdly, we want to promote the acquisition of talent in the public sector, especially in areas that are going to be definitive for the coming years, making progress in continuing training and in the professional development of public employees, which are the heart of the welfare state. Your ladies and gentlemen have at your disposal all the documentation and, therefore, I will only focus on some elements that I believe deserve to be underlined or highlighted. The total expenditure budget of our Ministry of Finance and Public Service, excluding the financing of autonomous communities and local entities, amounts to EUR 3341 million, an overall increase of 10.6%. In concrete terms, Chapter 1 includes items intended to increase the remuneration of public employees, which is carried out in a forecast of 2%, and also provides for the incorporation of new posts from the public offer of employment, namely 264 officials from the 2019 and 2020 offers which have already completed the selective processes and whose incorporation we expect to be made over the next year. Similarly, the reinforcement of the General Secretariat of European Funds and General Intervention in relation to the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism or, in other words, reinforcements that are motivated by the accompanying that we have to do for the deployment of all the recovery funds: 57 new posts for the IGAE and 85, as I say, for the recovery funds. As regards Chapter 2, current expenditure on goods and services, there is a 10% increase, reaching 89 million, motivated by actions related to the updating of the prices of cleaning services, also with security and telecommunications; items that are also intensified to cover corporate videoconferencing, which, as you know, have been installed in the public administration; probably to continue to intensify over the coming years. I would also like to point out the heading for the AIReF, both with regard to the tax monitoring rate, an element that guarantees its independence, which is increased by 1.21 million, and also in the resources we call the spending review, public expenditure review work, which amounts to almost 1 million euros. If you remember, the Council of Ministers itself has instructed the AIReF to carry out a third phase in the review of public expenditure, an element that must enable us to make further progress in efficiency and which is also committed to Brussels. The current transfers chapter as a whole is decreasing by EUR 4.6 million. In the part of external transfers, the departure of RTVE has logically been adapted, it has decreased, after the celebration of the Olympic Games, while there is no variation in the transfers to the consortia of Toledo, Cuenca, Santiago de Compostela and the Special Area Canaria. Credits for State property liability, which are provided with EUR 500 million, are also maintained invariably, basically to cover the processes resulting from the judgements relating to the health cent. For its part, the current internal transfers, that is, the State Mobile Park, the Institute of Fiscal Studies, the Tax Agency, the National Institute of Public Administration and the Transparency and Good Government Council, all of these items amount to 1140 million, an increase close to 28 million euros. The priority objective of the increase is to meet the operating costs of the staff of the Tax Agency, which is reinforced so that it can also further intensify the tasks of combating tax fraud. The chapter on real investments, Chapter 6, increases 12 million, up to 115.7, to meet obligations in relation to the reference value of real estate, which will be assumed by the Directorate-General for Disaster Management, and to renew computer equipment, cybersecurity project, interoperability or development of new applications in the field of management of European funds, as well as new investments in the public sector procurement platform. I would also like to point out the computer developments related to the Transparency and Open Government Portal to make accountability faster and an increasingly widespread practice. With regard to funding for territorial administrations, the budget is provided with €18 billion for the autonomous communities fund and another €3,000 for local authorities. They know that these funds currently have five compartments and that, compared to 2021, the total allocation is diminished due to the decrease in the reference of the deficit of autonomous communities for the year 2022 and that of the amount foreseen for the European Union React compartment, which, as you will recall, had a total of 12 billion, were distributed 10 000 in the year 2021 and around 2000 next year, hence the fall by this asymmetry in the distribution that occurs under this heading. On the other hand, the local entity fund is increased by $2 billion to cover needs estimated by the application of the Provider Payment Mechanism, which is regulated in these budgets for municipalities with special financial difficulties. To conclude this section, I would also like to highlight the actions of Service 50, which are in line with the Recovery Mechanism and which we have grouped under a single heading to make them more transparent and more visible. Specifically, there are 11 million in the digital transformation and modernization programme of the AGE, to modernize the Central Staff Register, to also improve the sensitivity, as it said, in relation to the Transparency Portal and to develop the Open Government, to launch a portal own for the Office of Conflicts of Interest, for the INAP to advance in automation and decentralization of selective processes and, finally, for Muface to also advance in administrative simplification with the implementation of the digital clinical history or the part of the electronic low. For its part, the Administration's transformation programme includes EUR 617 000 for the development of an information system that allows the Commission to monitor the fulfilment of milestones and objectives, also the creation of web portals that centralise information and improve the transparency of attendance and participation in the calls of individual entities, as well as the development of training activities to reach around 3000 employees of the public administration. The Digital Transformation and Modernisation of Territorial Entities Programme will carry out capital transfers of 239 million. In short, they are all items aimed at improving the efficiency of the public sector. Then, ladies and gentlemen, I would just like to comment that the centralised recruitment budget has an action that is more efficient, saving as a result of the aggregation of demand and the reduction of administrative burden. Just by giving them some data, so that they can see the impact that this policy is having, I will tell you that the average savings, for example, generated in the current telecommunications service contract is 37 million euros per year in line with greater efficiency. Similarly, the centralised cleaning contract over the next three years will also produce savings of EUR 15.8 million. The objective of next year is to continue this centralized procurement, and 334 million are allocated for contracts in postal communications and couriers, cleaning, telecommunications and security. We also foresee a provision of 20 million for the centralised purchase of non-emission vehicles, a purchase that complements that already made in the year 2021 in the amount of 70 million, but that, due to the shortage of semiconductors - one of the components that are essential in this manufacture of electric vehicles - there has been a delay in delivery, so that an item is entered to complete this purchase and meet this circumstance. Finally, with regard to section 38, I would like to highlight the Government's commitment to the financial adequacy of territorial administrations, because I believe that throughout the pandemic we have shown that we believe in the territorial state model on which the welfare state rests. We have done so with figures, and I therefore believe that the support of the Spanish Government for all the administrations is indisputable. And also for next year it remains so. In fact, by 2022 the autonomous communities will receive more than 126,500 million by adding up to 6,27 % more, plus the 7 billion corresponding to negative settlements and to the effect of the VAT information system. Remember that a fund of 7 billion has been set up that is aimed precisely at alleviating the financial burden of local governments and also of autonomous communities. In the first, the local entities, we have to incorporate 1228 million precisely to compensate for this liquidation and also the effect of the VAT information system. Thus, 7 billion plus 1228 make a total of 8228 million that increase for this item. That is the desire of this draft budget and of the sections that I have commented on today with your ladies and gentlemen so that they can receive their approval. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]
