ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-23-CD211123.u88	Thank you, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, speak before the plenary of this House to present the budget section for the Ministry of the Interior for the second time in this parliamentary term. First of all, I would like to reiterate once again at parliamentary level my thanks to the public employees belonging to the Ministry of the Interior, 192,000 women and men who, in the State Security Forces and Bodies, Prison Institutions, National Civil Protection System, Directorate-General for Traffic, Asylum and Refugee Office and, in general, in the other agencies that make up the department that I have the honour to lead, have done extraordinary work throughout this year 2021 in the face of equally extraordinary phenomena such as the pandemic, adverse weather events such as the Filomena earthquake, border crises such as the one in Ceuta or managing the arrival of refugees from Afghanistan. It is an extraordinary task, ladies and gentlemen, to which this House is not alien, because without the regulatory framework that you provide us and, above all, without the budgetary capacities that you approve annually, all these actions will be carried out with much more difficulty and possibly with less means. I would also like, Madam President, to express in these first words a message of solidarity for the people who live on the island of La Palma and who since last September have suffered the consequences of the eruption of the Old Cumbre volcano; a situation that has already led to the destruction of many private and public goods. To them and to them, to the neighbours of La Palma, therefore, a first message of solidarity and support. They have the Ministry of the Interior with them, they have the whole government supporting them and they have - I am convinced - the solidarity of this House that represents them. Ladies and gentlemen, the General Budgets of the State constitute the encrypted and accounting expression of three objectives pursued by the Government of Spain for the year 2022. First, to ensure a just recovery; a recovery that people have their real and main priority. These General Budgets of the State for 2022 consolidates a just economic and social recovery that will reach all the people and territories of our country and that implies the greatest expenditure of history on scholarships, pensions, dependency or social services. A recovery that drives the implementation of the 2030 Security Model, which is based no more or less than on the recovery of security as a public good, whose goal is to build a safety net in which, thanks to the work carried out by hundreds of thousands of public employees, each of us, each of us, can freely choose how he wants to live and how he wants to enjoy his well-being, his rights and his freedoms. To this end, Madam President, we present a section, the 16th, in which we increase the budget of the Ministry of the Interior by 5.11% and reach the 9632 million euros. It is an increase of 455 million euros compared to the year 2021 and contemplates some of the most important actions of this department in the last three years. First, the incorporation of 4843 personnel into the State Security Forces and Bodies during this year, of 2030 new public employees in Prison Institutions this year or the expansion of examiners in the General Directorate of Traffic. It also envisages the modernization and improvement of our equipment and means of action; for example, with EUR 4 200000 for the Ministry's Digital Plan, EUR 3 million for the modernisation of the processing of asylum applications, EUR 82 million for the renewal of the fleet of police and civil guard helicopters, EUR 15 400 000 for the Directorate-General for Civil Protection and Emergencies, EUR 8 200 000 for the renovation of infrastructure and police equipment and EUR 20 600 000 for the coverage of the Pegasus systems and the renewal of the vehicles of the mobile park of the Civil Guard Traffic Group. These are budgets on which we also rely on policies of dignity and the construction of the public memory of our society, as evidenced by the increase of 40 000 euros in the endowment of the Victims of Terrorism Foundation or the increase of 50 000 euros in subsidies to the entities of defence and representation of the victims of terrorism, precisely when ten years of the victory of democracy and the rule of law against the terrorist organisation ETA are fulfilled. Madam President, together with the just recovery, these General State Budgets are aligned with advances in equality and secure ecological and digital transition of Spanish society also in the Ministry of the Interior. Budget Section 16 will enable the 2030 Security Model to continue to materialise in three important areas. Firstly, by promoting the secure digitisation of our country with 40 250 000 euros of the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan that will be dedicated to actions in the field of digital identity and training of the Forces and Bodies of State Security and Prison Institutions or with 1 750 000 euros for the modernization of the management of electoral processes. Secondly, by aligning the ministry with the ecological transition, we can think of the almost 205 million euros that the Ministry of the Interior will receive, through credit transfer, for the realization of projects within the framework of the Energy Transition Plan in the General Administration of the State, of which more than 120 million will be allocated to buildings and almost 85 million to mobility projects. Finally, as it cannot be otherwise, ratifying our commitment to the social and territorial cohesion of our country, since these general budgets of 2022 allow to strengthen the incorporation of the demographic challenge into public security policies with measures as relevant as the commitment for the decentralized presence of agencies in the territory, being good proof of this the consolidation of the Center of Prison Studies in Cuenca or the endowment of 4 million euros for the creation of the university center of the National Police in Ávila. It also increased EUR 12 million in border infrastructure security, prison infrastructure and the implementation of the PNR system. Or almost EUR 44 million to strengthen the projects of the long-distance traffic management system. Some measures, ladies and gentlemen, which, in the area of digitisation, ecological transition and territorial equity, note the progress already expressed in Parliament on 17 February 2020 in securing the physical, digital, environmental or road environments in which we all develop our lives. Finally - I conclude, President - they are budgets with a clear vocation of generating public value. Yes, of public value generation. This progressive government of the Spanish and United Socialist Workers' Party We can, as you all know, commit to the President's investiture speech to strengthen the public services of the welfare state. Proof of this is the public job offers, which in the General Administration of the State have exceeded by 73% the offers made by the governments of the People's Party. Security is an area that, unfortunately, has not been freed from cuts or other practices by previous Governments. Therefore, since the motion of censure, the Government of Pedro Sánchez has opted for measures that will find their continuity in these budgets for the year 2022. The remuneration conditions of public employees will be improved by 2%, conditions that are preceded - ladies and gentlemen, it must be remembered - in the field of the State Security Forces and Bodies of the implementation in three sections of the equalization agreement that the People's Party signed and kept - yes, it kept - in a drawer. An Implementation that consolidated EUR 807 million last year in the payroll of the agents and involved an average increase of more than 20%. In addition, the General Budgets of the State will allow - as the Secretary of State for Security in the Internal Commission has already announced - to have a replenishment rate of 125 per cent for the Security Forces and Bodies, also to incorporate the staff of the Asylum and Refugee Office, as well as to modernize the infrastructures of the State security within the framework of the actions announced to them in their different appearances by the heads of the management centres of this Ministry of the Interior. Therefore - I conclude, Madam President - the budgets of this ministry are perfectly coordinated with the broad lines of the budgets for 2022. They enable us to advance the objectives of the planned and proposed coalition government in the investiture and are a key element in continuing the policy of implementing and deploying the 2030 security model, which, as I have told you on other occasions, is based on the recovery of security as a public good, the strengthening of our rights and freedoms, as well as the modernization of public security policies. A model fully aligned with the need to ensure a fair recovery - as it began - that will not leave anyone behind; fast, because the public bet will facilitate the exit from this crisis, and above all clean and exemplary in the behaviour of public officials. Because in the state budgets for 2022, far from increasing the number of agents destined for citizen security, they have been reduced by 3434 and 1738 for the National Police and Civil Guard, respectively. Nor will patrol cars be increased, now that our agents are going to devote themselves to taxiing. [[Applauds]] And what do we do with the identified subjects who are returned to the collection point? I will set an example for the Spanish to understand us, Minister of the Interior. A patrol observed a male in suspicious attitude wandering around in a playground. The call is received and the police come to identify him; there is no crime, only he comes. He is undocumented and transferred to the police station to identify him; he has a history of sexual assault and, with the reform, after de-identifying him, as he has not committed a crime, from now on what the police will have to do is to return him to that playground within a maximum of two hours, to see if by chance the children are still there and can commit some kind of crime against them, without there being any agents intended to monitor him because, of course, they will have to take other calls with the small number of troops they currently have. But, tell me, minister, because it is not included in your budgets, what happens if there is an accident in the displacement of this sexual predator? Who's gonna pay for it? Because nothing is included in your Section 16 accounts. Finally, and for lack of time, let's talk about anti-disturbing material, because agents are now forbidden to authorize rubber balls, something that is a last resort - as you know - on police devices, but that is essential in the face of street violence as a deterrent to avoiding the body by body. I conclude, Madam President, by addressing all the Spaniards, who are united by a common sense of law, order and authority, to ask them to join those police mobilizations apart from ideologies. This isn't a police issue, behind the thin line are you and your family.

[[Pause.-A cleaning service worker proceeds to disinfect the speaker's rostrum]] Section 17, Transport, Mobility and Urban Agenda We continue with the debate in section 17, Ministry of Transport, Mobility and Urban Agenda. For the presentation of the section, I have the floor on behalf of the Government, the Minister for Transport, Mobility and Urban Agenda.
ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-23-CD211123.u106	Thank you very much, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, I appear before this House to present to you the draft budgets of the Ministry of Transport, Mobility and Urban Agenda for the year 2022. This Government has prepared budgets for a fair recovery that will make Spain a more productive, more competitive and sustainable country and to ensure that economic improvement reaches all families. In this way, these budgets are part of a stage of growth in our economy which, together with the injection of European funds, gives us the opportunity to allocate resources to social and productive investment. [[The Lord]] The priority objectives that we have set ourselves in these budgets for the 2022 financial year in the Ministry are: to ensure sustainable, safe and efficient mobility for all citizens; to consider the right to decent and affordable housing as a reference and central value of all housing actions; and to improve the contribution of the transport sector to the achievement of the Sustainable Development Goals that our society needs, as the consequences of not addressing the challenge of sustainability are clearly being demonstrated. These are, therefore, budgets of impetus and modernisation, which will support economic growth and accelerate reforms that will allow us to be more resilient in social, economic and environmental terms; and this, because the ecological and digital transition are key to the sustainability of our productive model and the quality of life of citizens. In addition, these are budgets that promote equality, territorial cohesion and cohesion between people to ensure equal opportunities regardless of where they were born or lived. On the basis of these premises, I will then give you some specific details of the budget submitted by the Ministry of Transport, Mobility and Urban Agenda for 2022. The group's consolidated budget for 2022 in chapters 4, 6, 7 and 8 and investments by the entities amounts to EUR 19,328 million, an increase of 14.9% over the current budgets. Of this amount, the amount earmarked for investment is growing even more, 17 %, reaching 16 286 million. They are, therefore, the continuation of a radical change of trend that we have already started last year. Together, they represent a determined commitment to recovery, but without leaving anyone behind, giving continuity to the effort begun in last year's budgets to mitigate the impact of the biggest health crisis in our history. This investment effort is guaranteed by the funds from the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan approved by the European Commission, which is a key lever for speeding up the above-mentioned processes. In the budgets for 2022 the funds from this mechanism amount to a total of €65 billion. Thanks to this plan we will accelerate the implementation of policies for which we are committed to mobility and housing. In particular, the major items of the plan are to decarbonize our cities, to co-ordinate, connect and modernise mobility and housing policy through the creation of a real social rental housing park and for the rehabilitation of the existing building park. It is a key part of economic recovery and its job creation capacity. We are therefore faced with a ministry whose responsibility is capital, as the investment we plan to make represents about 40% of public investment in budgets and 1.3% of gross domestic product and will involve the creation of about 350,000 jobs, many in sectors with a high capacity for future development. And when we talk about sustainability we do it seriously, more than 54% of the investment and capital transfers from the ministry's budget have green labels. They are budgets geared to the citizens and companies that form the productive fabric of our country, which focus on their needs and on the incorporation of mechanisms to deal with the changes that we are facing. In detailing what we are going to do with these budgets, I would like to focus first on housing policy and the urban agenda, as these budgets represent a change in the order of magnitude of the figures that this ministry invested in other stages of government. For you to come up with an idea, we multiply the budget inherited from 2018 by seven. It has never invested so much in rehabilitation and public promotion to respond to what is a social emergency, for which it is a peremptory need of many families and thousands and thousands of young people, whose emancipation we will support with programs such as the young rental bonus. The importance we attach to these policies is evident in the budgetary effort they concentrate, with an increase of 46.8% over the current budget. Thus, we have allocated EUR 3289 million to housing and urban agenda - an increase of EUR 1048 million - which is the largest budget in history in this area. Our priorities in this area will be, first of all, the management of the new State Plan for Access to Housing 2022-2025, which will have 425 million euros in the coming year, as signed with each of the autonomous communities. Secondly, the young rental voucher, which will have a budget of 200 million euros. In addition, the Housing Plan for Affordable Rent and Other Shares, which will have 144 million euros. But, in addition to the funds of the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism, a total of EUR 2489 million is allocated to housing policies for rehabilitation programmes for economic and social recovery in residential environments, with EUR 1389 million; for the construction of social rental housing in energy-efficient buildings, with EUR 500 million, and stressing that with this amount more will be allocated during 2023 the construction of 20,000 social rental housing will be promoted. Also from the funds correspond to the sustainable and digital rehabilitation of public buildings for local entities, 600 million euros, and finally, the policies of urban agenda and architecture that will have 32 million euros, destined to give continuity to the commitment of recovery and enrichment of our monumental historical heritage. I would also like to go on to detail the main transport and infrastructure items. As your ladies and gentlemen know, the other great challenge of our ministry's actions is the transformation of our mobility to make it safer, more sustainable, more inclusive and more digital. The transport sector is a strategic sector for the overall development of the country's economy; a sector that guarantees the mobility of citizens, responds to the free movement of goods and is a basic tool for increasing the productivity of the country's economy. It is also a fundamental pillar for achieving the environmental objectives of a modern and developed society. Also in this case, the funds of the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism provide a fundamental part of the appropriations to finance the actions we present. The most relevant figures in this area are as follows. Almost EUR 1285 million from European funds will be earmarked to support investments to be made by local authorities, the autonomous communities and companies, both public and private. In particular, EUR 951 million will be devoted to the deployment of low-emission areas in urban and metropolitan environments and to the digital and sustainable transformation of urban transport and mobility. EUR 150 million has also been planned for the transformation of fleets of private passenger and freight transport companies, EUR 172 million related to digitisation of both administrative services and transport companies, and EUR 11 million to boost the training of public transport and logistics professionals. Together with these actions financed by European funds, we must not forget the maintenance of the instruments which have traditionally formed the fundamental part of transport policy. Thus, we also include in the budgets EUR 321 million to finance grants to consortia, metropolitan authorities and local authorities to finance urban collective transport. In addition, an additional EUR 51 million is needed to subsidise urban collective transport in other municipalities, including the financing of public service obligations, be they rail, air and sea, so as to ensure the viability of these services, which will have a budget of more than EUR 1253 million in 2022. Similarly, air and sea ticket bonuses for residents of non- peninsular territories will have an appropriation of EUR 783 million in 2022 to finance 75 % of the price of air and boat tickets for residents of non- peninsular territories. For their part, also the bonuses to large families in the rail, air and sea transport will have a budget of slightly more than 24 million euros. In addition, subsidies for the transport of goods with the extra-penisular territories will have a budget of more than EUR 74 million. Nor would I want to forget in this chapter the slightly more than EUR 92.5 million intended to meet the reductions in tolls on state highways, maintaining those currently in force and encouraging the introduction of new reductions. Turning briefly to the different modes of transport, the railway is the mode of transport that leads the investments planned for the next year with an investment of 6924 million euros, an amount that accounts for almost 42% of the total investments of this ministry and which is increased by 13,6% compared to the budget of 2021. In this amount, the items intended for the development of new lines to complement the Trans-European Transport Network are highlighted. Here I must remind you that both the Mediterranean and Atlantic Corridors respond to a country strategy to improve our rail connection with the rest of Europe in a balanced manner throughout the Iberian Peninsula, which is reflected in the General Budgets of the State, with more than EUR 17 billion earmarked for the Mediterranean Corridor and EUR 14 billion for the Atlantic. Also the adaptation and improvement of the conventional network, with special dedication to the metropolitan transport of surroundings, to which we allocate more than 1500 million euros. In this sense, we have a clear commitment to daily mobility, the transformation of urban and proximity transport, with the aim of making it an attractive and reliable public transport as a backbone of urban mobility. Also the improvement of the connections for the traffic of goods, where the railway is called to be a fundamental actor thanks to its sustainability,so we increase investments in conventional network by 55%. For its part, for the investment corresponding to roads, a total of 2463 million. I would like to highlight the provision for maintenance and maintenance operations, which amounts to EUR 1371 million. This is the largest conservation budget in history for the State's road network. It represents an 11 % growth over the 2021 budget and an accumulated 75 % growth over the 2018 extended budget. As far as the maritime mode is concerned, investments reach just over EUR 1 billion and have as their priority objectives the improvement of rail connectivity, environmental and energy sustainability or digitisation and innovation. All this, not to mention the investment in improving our maritime rescue and pollution control system. In the air mode, the planned investment amounts to EUR 836 million, and I would like to stress that these figures include the forecasts of DORA II 2022-2026, with which we aim to recover traffic and efficient management of the airport network, environmental sustainability and innovation as an essential part for efficiency and quality in the provision of services. At the same time, in the field of air navigation, we also continue to make progress in digitisation, automation and technological impetus. I should like to conclude, ladies and gentlemen, by stressing some of the most important figures in the budgets that I am presenting to you today, with which we will help to consolidate the recovery of our economy. They contain a historic public investment of 16286 million euros because this ministry is key to our country's economic recovery. We have a total of €6.5 billion from the European Union Recovery and Resilience Mechanism for urban rehabilitation and regeneration, sustainable mobility, digitisation, intermodality and logistics. They are just budgets for an inclusive recovery for all and for all, which target housing policies and urban agenda almost €33 billion to combat exclusion, help the emancipation of young people, promote rehabilitation and combat energy poverty. They are budgets that drive daily mobility, the sustainable transformation of urban and proximity transport, betting on proximity and also on the high speed and conventional network that unites and connects territories and people. These are, in short, budgets for a decentralized and coordinated Spain, as demonstrated by the more than €36 billion in transfers to the autonomous communities and local entities, which have been earmarked for sustainable mobility projects, rehabilitation and social housing. These are budgets for the safe, sustainable and connected transformation, which are truly committed to the conservation and safety of our road network, our infrastructure network, which drive the digitisation of transport and move towards environmental sustainability and the fight against climate change, because more than 54% of the investment in this budget, I insist, according to the criteria set by Europe, is green-labelled investment. In short, they are budgets for the citizens of our country to be able to improve their housing, their building, their neighbourhood, their city or to see their mobility opportunities facilitated and improved. Finally, I would also like to express my thanks to those political formations which, during the course of the draft law, have expressed their willingness to support these General State Budgets for the next financial year of 2022 and which have been translated into, and are being translated into, the adoption of amendments. Thank you so much. [[Applauds]]

And the third priority is the digitization of our education system. To this end, EUR 477 million will be allocated, representing 7.9% of the total. Obviously, the planning and implementation of these investments will be largely done with the Autonomous Communities through the various territorial cooperation programmes. Moreover, as your ladies and gentlemen know, since the beginning of this pandemic, the Spanish Government has also been at the side of the autonomy in this crisis, with an extraordinary economic endowment. In 2020, we have allocated slightly more than EUR 16.5 billion, 2000 of which are dedicated solely and exclusively to education, and this year we have also allocated more than EUR 13.5 billion to communities to safeguard public services, including education. You will understand that, even if it is not the subject of this budget, you will also take advantage of this small intervention to thank once again for the enormous and exemplary effort that the whole educational community is making to deal with the pandemic. [[Applauds]] I will detail below the main specific expenditure programmes planned for the next financial year. As you know, the Ministry's activities now include three spending policies: education policy, employment promotion policy, foreign policy and development cooperation. In the latter field, educational action abroad, although less well known, represents a valuable activity for the dissemination of Spanish culture, education and language. It will have 123 million euros by 2022. Within education policy, programmes designed to promote as much equity as possible are an undisputed priority; equity to make it possible for all to access the highest level of training without being affected by economic, social, access or learning barriers. To this end, we focus on scholarship policies, inclusion policies, and on continuing to take measures to reduce early school leaving. The policy of scholarships and study grants, as a fundamental tool for ensuring educational equity, has been a priority for this Government, as it shows that the resources devoted to scholarships have increased by 44% over four years. Of the €1493 million of the 2017 budget year, we will spend in the year 2022 to €2149 million. This means that almost a million families, 986,000 students, will benefit from a scholarship with the current call; and that is 200,000 more young people, as I say, than in the call for 2017. It is a record investment aimed at covering the entry of new beneficiaries as a result of the pandemic crisis. I would also like to emphasise the EUR 50 million earmarked for the aid of textbooks. Education inclusion policies highlight a new territorial cooperation programme, with €43 million, aimed at significantly increasing the resources and means to make progress in improving the care of pupils with special educational needs. Also, between the actions aimed at equity and the quality of our education system, next year will have a relevant role several programs included in the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan, with a total of 494 million euros. I would like to highlight the EUR 334 million earmarked for the creation of 21 794 public child education places, mainly for children of one and two years of age, because it is a measure that translates very directly into real equality; a measure that will allow the creation of public and free places that will allow many families not to have to leave their professional career because they cannot cope with the expenditure of a day care centre. Or the implementation of 2500 centers of the PROA program, endowed with 120 million euros. And, on the other hand, the creation of at least a thousand units of personal and family support and guidance of the student's educationally vulnerable, with 39 million euros. In the field of vocational training, as I have already told you - and you know it perfectly well, as the organic law on vocational training is being dealt with in this House - the Government has identified as one of its priorities in the legislature the promotion and modernisation of vocational training. The total budget for 2022 in vocational training amounts to EUR 2299.1 million, with an increase of EUR 660 million over the previous year. The main action programmes will be the boost and improvement of vocational training in the education system, with 275.4 million euros; programmes associated with the Modernisation of Vocational Training Plan, with a total of 872.3 million euros; or digital transformation, which will be financed with 232.6 million euros. I would like to stress that among all these actions, in addition, there is a small programme in economic amount, with 3 million euros, but with a great social impact, such as the Mentor Classrooms; in 2022, we planned to open at least 750 new classes of this type. And the third major pillar of vocational training activities in this budget is training for employment, both for the employed and the unemployed, with EUR 1151.2 million. The planned investments in vocational training in 2022 will enable, among other objectives, the creation of 50,485 new vocational training places within the project of those 200,000 to which we have committed ourselves over the next four years, the creation or conversion of 700 groups into bilinguals, the creation of 221 new technology classrooms, the launch of 450 entrepreneurship classrooms and digital training for at least 125,000 workers. The third line of action of this ministry focuses on the digitization of the education system. Ladies and gentlemen, you know very well that as a result of this pandemic, our capacities and also our lack of resources for non-personal education have been tested. Thanks to European funding, we have been able to give great impetus to the digitisation of education in Spain. By 2022, the budget will be EUR 284 million with the aim of strengthening above all digital skills and educational resources. In addition, the specific digitisation items for vocational training should be added to this figure, so that the total amount of resources earmarked for digitisation of our education system will exceed EUR 477 million. I would also like to mention, ladies and gentlemen, the budget estimates for Ceuta and Melilla, autonomous cities where educational management is carried out directly by this ministry. Educational management in both autonomous cities, which affects several budget programmes, includes a budget of EUR 186.7 million. I'm concluding, ladies and gentlemen. The year 2021 and the year 2022 will represent, if this budget comes forward in the terms proposed, a historical milestone of investment in our educational system for its modernization and a budgetary effort that is allowing us to face the main challenges that our educational system has: school dropout, inequality, economic segregation or the lack of qualification of professionals that our productive fabric needs. We are doing this with rules but also with investments. This Government fulfils its word and has clear priorities: to strengthen public services, to ensure equality and to promote the modernization of the economy and the productive fabric in order to have quality jobs. Ladies and gentlemen, having different positions on the educational model in our country is not incompatible with recognising the social value of the very significant improvements that this budget makes in terms of scholarships, in terms of digitisation, in terms of educational reinforcement for those who need it most, in terms of inclusion or in deep and renewed vocational training. Therefore, I invite you to join in this process of improvement to build that climate of trust and understanding that the educational community is waiting for, and therefore request your support for these budgets for the year 2022. Thank you so much. [[Applauds]]

What we are asking of you, Minister, is that this change in economic paradigm also means a change in educational paradigm, that we are able to address the educational transformation that this country needs, which is already essential. There are five important measures, Madam Minister. The first is to ensure the deployment of 0-3 education, which is an educational stage. This is reflected in the budgets with the creation of more than 60,000 public places from 0 to 3. The second, progressive expansion of public places in all stages of education, because Lomlou also says so. The third, a reduction in ratios, is the commitment to inclusive education and, also, to individualized care in our education system. The fourth, an inclusion plan, is budgeted with more than 43 million euros, and must allow the school to truly be the school of all, of all and for all. And the fifth, to bet on the formation in values, to incorporate elements LGTB, of democratic memory, of sustainability, of fair taxation in the educational curriculum. It should also be accompanied by teacher training and other professional profiles that interact within the classroom with the student. In short, we have done our duties in two areas: we have a new law that designs this transformation of our educational system, and we will have new expansionary budgets that will guarantee the investments necessary for that law to be a reality. Now we just need to confirm that what we have agreed, what we have agreed, what we have budgeted will end up fulfilling and I ended up landing in the classrooms of our schools. You began, Minister, by thanking the educational community for its efforts in recent years of management of COVID in the classrooms. Evidently we, from Unidad Podemos and In Comú Podemos, join in that thank you and we are also clear that the best way to thank you is by shielding public education, guaranteeing sufficient resources and strengthening the working conditions of all educational staff, for example, also of civil servants who have been at the foot of the canyon during the management of the COVID, because there is nothing more transformative than education. Let us, Minister, make these budgets the engine of change that our educational system also needs. Thank you. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-23-CD211123.u152	Good afternoon, President, ladies and gentlemen. I would like to begin by thanking your ladies and gentlemen for their contribution to the budgets that are already in this House, for the debate and for their contribution to enriching the general budgets of the 2022 state, in particular the parliamentary groups participating in this great country agreement, which goes far beyond this government. These budgets mean a decisive advance in the role that public authorities must play in the development of our country and the progress of our society. The leap produced in 2021 is consolidated, as you will see, in the year 2022. These budgets strengthen services and public investment as a lever for recovery and undertake significant transformations pending in our country. They are not limited to a moment of exception in the face of a crisis, they are a project for the future. Obviously, in a budget not only the amount, but the for what and, most importantly, the for whom. In the case of the consolidated budget of the Ministry of Labour and Social Economy we show the commitment to quality employment, decent work and the role that work must play in the fair recovery and modernization of our country. The main objective of the budgets presented to you today is to respond to these structural problems, while facing a just recovery after the worst global pandemic in a century, with the social and economic crisis it brought with it, and the data support our strategy. We have been reducing unemployment for eight consecutive months, accumulating a decline in unemployment of more than 752,000 people since February. Of the 3 617 000 people who were at ERTE in April 2020, 3,477,000 have already joined their jobs and fewer than 90,000 with ERTE in full-time suspension. If at the hardest time of the pandemic, in confinement, we reach 25% of the wage-earner population protected by an ERTE, it is now less than 1%. The Ministry of Labour and Social Economy will have a consolidated budget of EUR 30 142 million, certainly a high figure reflecting that employment is one of the central priorities of the Government of Spain. In comparative terms, it is 7.6% lower than last year's budget thanks to the positive evolution of employment, which reduces spending needs to meet public unemployment benefits, especially in the area of ERT. With this second budget of the legislature we definitely leave behind the austerity and cuts of the previous government and move in the opposite direction: more rights for citizens, better services and better public benefits, as well as a strong boost to economic activity and employment. Let me give a brief explanation of the figures and priorities for action in this budget. Firstly, the redesign and implementation of active employment policies is one of the backbones of our ministerial strategy, reaching the amount of EUR 6480 million, a very high figure, as you know, which consolidates the extraordinary increase of 35.6% we made last year. The new generation of active employment policies ensures the personalisation of services, focused both on individuals and on enterprises, with a view to strengthening the number of professionals. Modernisation of services implies incorporation into the digital age, increasing and making more accessible employment services and putting artificial intelligence at the service of work and employment in our country. We will better and better protect the unemployed by strengthening the coverage of the system, which in the 2022 financial year will have 22,093 million euros for public benefits. The lower need for ERTE benefits, as I have told you, makes it possible to reduce overall expenditure, but I must also tell you that in 2022 the resources for unemployment benefit will be increased by 5.47 %, precisely in anticipation of the reform of the welfare part of the protection system that we have incorporated into component 23. We will continue to significantly improve the level of protection for the most vulnerable by increasing the IPREM to 2.5%, with a budgetary impact of EUR 250 million, which will improve the level of benefit for more than one and a half million people. As you know, ladies and gentlemen, IPREM has been practically frozen since 2010. In fact, between 2010 and 2020 only EUR 5 was raised, and it is only that in these two budget years we have raised it by EUR 41. And just as we have raised the minimum wage, the increase in IPREM means raising the basic threshold for the benefits of those who need it most. If we do not turn our backs on precariousness and cuts, as I say to you, we reach out to our youth, because it will be the one that builds the recovery and reconstruction of our country, in short, that allows us also to address this crisis; I think it cannot be otherwise, it is our talent and our possibility. These budgets will enable progress to be made in appropriate labour integration policies, especially for women and young people. The tools are set out for innovative treatment of training processes, as you know, in alternation with work activity. Alongside the development of existing employment programmes, there is a strong commitment to the return of emigrant talent, as well as to young employment plans in research programmes. In particular, under component 23 we incorporated the Investigó, Tandem and First Employment Opportunity plans for young people. We do so because investing in training and talent remains the guarantee of employment and opportunities; in short, they are the best dike against inequality. The ministry has a very significant participation in the Plan of Reconstruction, Transformation and Resilience of the Spanish economy, in this case with an amount of 807.6 million euros in this year's budget to advance in an economic model based on quality employment. It is also very important that we have the autonomous communities, and that is why we will consolidate in the year 2022 the extraordinary increase in the amount of funds transferred to them, as was done in the past budget year, so that they can better develop their competences in active employment policies, advancing in a model of shared governance, in which, as you know, plays a relevant role the Sectoral Conference on Employment and Labour Affairs, and are amounts never known in Spanish democracy. We will strengthen the Labour and Social Security Inspectorate, the fundamental instrument to ensure compliance with the law, as you know, another institution cut off by the Government of the People's Party and which will have a budget that will reach almost 200 million euros, which will allow us to strengthen the capacity of action in the different categories of the inspection activity, ultimately complying with the strategic plan of the agency that we have presented these days. We are developing a labour inspection at the service of the social state, which is very important, understanding that it is one of the fundamental pillars of healthy, effective and decent labour relations, because, as we say, not only do we need good laws, but we need the laws to be enforced. We will also strengthen the Public Employment Service, increasing its Chapter 1 by 3.65%, in order to meet the needs of its staff, and we will also increase investment by 29.7% directly aimed at digitisation, with a special focus on European funds; a body which, as you know, was also strongly cut by the People's Party government. In the area of self-employment, we are going to deploy the National Self-Employment Impulse Strategy, developed in dialogue with the sector's entities. I have to tell you that it is on the way out and we have a budget increase of up to EUR 15 million. Similarly, we have and deploy the National Strategy for Social Economy 2021-2027, agreed as a road map for a sector that represents 10% of GDP and 12% of workers in our country, so that it can deploy its transformative potential for generating and distributing wealth. Moreover, as you already know, the 2022 budget will allow us to continue the scheme of protection of ERTs, the result of the social dialogue, until the end of February next year, but with the will of this Government to extend and maintain them whenever necessary. However, as you know, we are committed that from 1 January the ERTEs will be the structural response to changes in the economic cycle, that is to say, it is one of the major changes that are being made at the social dialogue table. These budgets go one step further. We are going to modernise and Europeanise our labour policies, and this is reflected in the mutual commitment between the European Union and the Government of Spain. We want to place work on the basis of a new social contract that promotes inclusive growth and shared prosperity, which is good for businesses and also for working people. And here I come back to the beginning: it matters the for what, and that is why we link the ongoing recovery to the pending transformations, opening up a horizon of prosperity for all and for all, and it matters the for whom, because this budget is designed to reach all households, especially those who need it most. One of the words you have spoken in this crisis is resilience. We have learned the importance of strengthening equality and public services for when they are wronged. These budgets promote equality between men and women, between young and old, between cities and the countryside, between those who have enough and, ultimately, those who need it. We rebuild the state of well-being for the twenty-first century, promote the modernization of the country and an inclusive and sustainable development, where wealth is distributed with justice and production and consumption are careful with our land. During the pandemic we were clear that we should all go out together and not leave anyone behind, now is the time for recovery to reach everyone. The effectiveness and justice of budgets are measured in the development and social progress they produce. It is the time to bet on the public to guarantee the protection and rights of all citizens, but it is also the time to promote a wealth creation that improves the lives of all families so that growth among all households and, ultimately, we have general budgets in favour of our country and our people. That is why I ask you to support them. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

[[Pause.-A cleaning service worker proceeds to disinfect the speaker's rostrum]] Section 20, Industry, Trade and Tourism We move on to the debate in section 20, Ministry of Industry, Trade and Tourism. For the section presentation, I have the floor on behalf of the Government, the Minister for Industry, Trade and Tourism.
ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-23-CD211123.u174	Good afternoon, President. Ladies and gentlemen, it is an honour for me to attend the plenary session of the Congress of Deputies to explain the broad lines of the draft budgets for 2022 of the Ministry of Industry, Commerce and Tourism. In my speech, I highlight the large figures in the budget, but also the priority orientations of spending policies. The first reflection I would like to convey to all of you is that the Ministry of Industry, Trade and Tourism will have the largest budget in its history, a budget aimed at consolidating the just economic recovery, but also at driving the process of transforming and modernising our productive fabric. With this budget, the ministry strengthens productive investment among sectors that are essential to accelerate the transformation of the Spanish economy, such as industry, trade and tourism. This public support will boost private activity in key areas such as sustainability, digitalisation and innovation, but also social and territorial cohesion, with a strong boost from small and medium-sized enterprises. As you know, this budget incorporates the plans and projects linked to the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism adopted by the European Union through the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan that was approved last June. This is undoubtedly a collective success of the European Union, in which the Spanish Government played a leading role. It is a budget, that of 2022, which amounts to EUR 8300 million, which means, ladies and gentlemen, an increase of EUR 3402 million, 70% higher than the current budget. Of this amount, a total of €4876 million is allocated to the funds of the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism. Next, I will explain an orderly presentation of the three areas of spending in my department. The area of industry and SMEs will have €5735 million in 2022, a historic increase over this year's budget of more than 95,8 %. Without doubt, this budget is a unique and probably unrepeatable opportunity, ladies and gentlemen, to make an industrial and SME policy that we need and that we have been yearning for for for years in our country. The most important line of this budget is support for strategic projects for economic recovery and transformation, the so-called PERTE. As you know, this year we approved in June the electric and connected vehicle PART, a PART which has a multiannual budget of 2975 million euros, being allocated, for the year 2022, 1660 million euros. Also, ladies and gentlemen, we are going to strengthen next year two programmes which are very relevant to the industrial sector and which allow the financing of innovation projects, organisational processes and R & D projects, a programme with EUR 150 million, an increase of 58% compared to the programme convened this year. There are also projects for the implementation of digitization in processes and organization of industrial companies, a program with 150 million euros, which means an increase of 60% of the call that we have approved this year. Ladies and gentlemen, in the area of SMEs, we are going to develop a number of actions that I will describe. As regards the promotion of entrepreneurship, the School of Industrial Organisation and Enisa will develop actions within the framework of the Spain Entrepreneur Nation Strategy with more than 30 million euros. In the area of business growth, the allocation for the guarantee refinancing system will be strengthened by providing the technical provisions fund of some EUR 113 million. In addition, we are going to launch a new programme, the industrial entrepreneurship programme, which provides guarantees for the financing of industrial SMEs; a programme which will be endowed with 30 million euros and which will be articulated through mutual guarantee companies, thus benefiting from the financial costs of guarantees requested by industrial SMEs for the creation of new industrial establishments or for projects of change of scale in production in the company. The School of Industrial Organisation will also strengthen actions to increase the growth capacities of our SMEs, with a budget of 40 million euros. And I would also like to highlight the Innovative Business Groups programme, the IEA programme, a programme that is being updated, focusing on the digital transformation of industry, and which for 2022 has an investment of EUR 50 million, which means, ladies and gentlemen, an increase of more than one hundred per cent of what we have budgeted this year. Apart from the European funds, from the Resilience Mechanism, I would like to highlight the increase in the indirect cost compensation programme, which by the year 2022 will be endowed with 179 million euros and, in addition, to transfer them that in the normative part of the 2022 State General Budgets it is planned to increase this item with revenues from greenhouse gas emission allowances auctions in the terms approved in the Climate Change Act, which will allow, ladies and gentlemen, to meet the maximum of the applications received. To give you a given, in this year's call for CO2 aid, we have met 93% of the applications received and 170 companies and 210 production centres have benefited. In addition, the provision for compensation of charges provided for in the Statute for Electrointensive Consumers, with EUR 91 million, is maintained, and the Fund for Support of Industrial Productive Investment, which, as you know, is replenished with an additional EUR 600 million for long-term strategic financing of the growth of industrial enterprises, is replenished. In short, ladies and gentlemen, these budgets are a unique opportunity for our country's industry, to speed up its adaptation to the dual green and digital transition, but also to ensure that industry continues to contribute to the creation of wealth, to the creation of quality jobs and to increase its competitiveness. In the area of tourism, we will have EUR 1800 million, an increase of 36% over the budget of 2021, a figure that represents a historical volume destined by the General Administration of the State to modernise our tourism sector. This spending policy aims to contribute to the revival of tourism, while addressing the major transformations that will enable us to maintain competitiveness and strengthen our leadership at the international level. To this end, we will establish five priority areas, in which we will develop different programmes in coordination with the Autonomous Communities, local authorities and the private sector. The first of these priority areas, ladies and gentlemen, is the transformation of tourism supply towards sustainability. The call for tourism sustainability plans for destination is increased by 9%, reaching €720 million for next year. As you will know, today we have approved in the Council of Ministers the extraordinary call for 2021, with an investment of 615 million euros, a call for which we have received more than five hundred sustainability plans, indicating the great welcome this programme has received. I announce that before the end of the year we will approve the seventeen territorial plans. The second of our priorities is the commitment to digitisation and tourism intelligence. The digital transformation of our destinations will count to 115 million euros, 80% more than in 2021. The investment priorities addressed in this area are, on the one hand, the consolidation of our tourist intelligence system, a unique environment of access to information between the supply and demand of the Spanish tourism sector, which is added from destination to national level. Secondly, we will continue to develop the intelligent destination platform, which will provide interoperability with the provision of public and private services for the tourist; a platform that will allow all agents to relate to the tourist in a smart and scalable digital way. As you know, last week we announced that the headquarters of this platform will be in Benidorm, a pioneering destination, a destination that has been the first to obtain DTI certification, and it is also a way of continuing to decentralize the services of the General Administration of the State, because this government - yes, ladies and gentlemen - is believed to have to address the demographic challenge, and to do so with facts, not words, and this is certainly good news. In the area of digitisation, the budgets for 2022 also include EUR 45 million in the Programme for the transformation and innovation of tourism enterprises. In particular, we will call for aid for preferably small and medium-sized tourism companies, to finance innovative technology-based projects, as well as the development of a comprehensive digital skills plan that supports this transformation. Finally and in this same axis, it is important to highlight the actions that Segittur will do to support the Network of Smart Tourist Destinations, a network of which 347 institutions that make up the main tourist destinations of Spain are currently part. The promotion of the digital tourism ecosystem, internationalization and marketing, national tourism services and resources will be part of the strategy that Segittur develops throughout 2022. The third of our priorities are tourism resilience strategies in extrapenisular territories, strategies that are developed in the Canary Islands and the Balearic Islands, which are most affected by the impact of the pandemic. In collaboration with the autonomous governments, each of the archipelagos, ladies and gentlemen, will have EUR 50 million, an increase of 150% over the strategy that we are going to adopt this year. In 2022, the strategy of resilience of the Canary Islands announced that it will take into account the recovery of the island of La Palma. From here, again, shows all the affection and support for palm trees and palm trees, but also for its tourism sector, which will count in the year 2022 with a recovery plan of this sector so important for the island. The fourth priority is the improvement of the competitiveness of our tourism sector, on the basis of a programme that we are going to approve this week, a programme of tourism experiences Spain, which doubles in the year 2022 the allocation for this year, counting on a credit of 49 million euros. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, I would also like to highlight the tourist promotion that Turespana is developing. Until the end of 2022 he will devote his resources to recovering the demand for quality international tourism to Spain. In short, ladies and gentlemen, we are going to make an investment effort as never before to contribute to the recovery, but also to the modernization, of our tourism model, and to consolidate a more sustainable, diversified tourism, committed to the demographic challenge, and also more digital and inclusive. In the area of trade, the strong impact of the pandemic has undoubtedly accelerated as a result of the trends we observed before it, and it is these trends that represent our main strategic lines. Support for internationalisation, the boost to the competitiveness of Spanish companies, especially small and medium-sized enterprises, as well as the digital transformation and sustainability of retail trade, are some of the priorities that we will reinforce with the budgets of 2022. At present, we are seeing a significant increase in exports of goods. Today the data for the first nine months of the year have been published, with an increase of 22 %, but the most important thing is that exports already exceed, ladies and gentlemen, the pre-crisis records, despite the problems in supplies of some components or the rise in the price of raw materials. The Government's internationalization strategy is reflected in Action Plan 2021-2022, whose actions seek to achieve an export sector that increases its resilience and is configured as what it already is: a pillar of growth and job creation. For this, we will count on more than 200 million euros this year, coming from the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan, programs that focus, ladies and gentlemen, especially on small and medium-sized enterprises, because it is the SMEs that face the most difficulties to go abroad. In the area of retail trade, there are also initiatives to boost their competitiveness, digital transformation, the sustainability of retail trade, which have been implemented in three lines of aid and the creation of a specific fund for the trade sector for the next year. The three lines of aid, which I am going to describe to you, have, first of all, the sustainable markets programme, a programme aimed at financing projects for the improvement and modernisation of municipal markets, commercial areas, non-sedentary markets and short marketing channels. It is a programme with €66.6 million for 2022. The second of the programmes, which we are developing in collaboration with the Spanish Federation of Municipalities and Provinces, is approved for municipalities with less than 5000 inhabitants; a programme with 5 million euros, especially, as I say, aimed at areas of lower population density to strengthen the activity and commercial distribution through the use of technologies that allow to generate synergies between municipalities, between shops and neighbours, facilitating the consumption and delivery of the products. Again, a measure to meet the demographic challenge. Thirdly, there is the programme to strengthen commercial activity in tourist municipalities, a programme endowed with €33.3 million, which aims to finance plans and programmes aimed at the introduction of improvements in commercial areas of tourist areas that have suffered a strong negative impact as a result of the pandemic. These lines, ladies and gentlemen, give continuity to those published this year in the BOE on 11 October and which are at that time in a state of resolution. We have received, ladies and gentlemen, more than 749 requests, which shows the great acceptance that the three lines have had, even tripling the value and economic investment with which we counted in 2021. In 2022, ladies and gentlemen, we will once again draw up these three calls for support for retail trade and, as I told you, we will incorporate a new instrument, the technological fund, which will be endowed with 50 million euros, a fund that will provide small and medium-sized traders who will be willing to invest in improvements in their commercial establishments with technological and innovative criteria, in order to increase their competitiveness and accelerate digitisation towards a more sustainable model. Ladies and gentlemen, as I have been able to transfer to you, the funds associated with the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan represent almost 60% of the budget of the Ministry of Industry, Commerce and Tourism. We certainly have a great responsibility ahead of us to effectively manage the large amount of resources that we are going to mobilize to modernize and transform our productive fabric, and we are going to do so, ladies and gentlemen - and we are doing so - by collaborating with the private sector, but also by the hand of autonomous communities and local entities. I would like to end with a final reflection. After the months so hard that we have lived since the beginning of the pandemic, there are reasons for hope and we must be proud of what we have achieved as a country. We are effectively controlling the pandemic through the vaccination process. As mentioned earlier, social security membership is at its peak and we have already regained the jobs that existed before the pandemic. The economic recovery, ladies and gentlemen, is a fact, it is under way, and everything indicates that Spain will be one of the countries that will grow the most in Europe next year. In addition, ladies and gentlemen, we will continue to protect employment and our productive fabric whatever it takes. Today in the Council of Ministers we approved an extension of the moratorium on competitions until June 2022. Protecting employment and our productive fabric, but also investing in generating employment and modernizing our productive fabric, are the two recipes that the Government brings in these budgets. Adopting these budgets, ladies and gentlemen, will enable us to implement the reforms and transformations that this country needs thanks to an ambitious road map that we are launching through the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan, and it is the responsibility of all the parliamentary groups that this draft budgets, ladies and gentlemen, come forward. Its approval is not only important to support the productive fabric, to generate employment or to strengthen our welfare state, it is also undoubtedly a very important message of confidence and stability that will strengthen Spain's credibility. That is why I thank the parliamentary groups that are going to support these budgets and I appeal to the other groups to join this project, which will allow us, as I say, to consolidate the recovery but, above all, to facilitate the modernization and transformation of our country. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

[[Pause.-A cleaning service worker proceeds to disinfect the speaker's rostrum]] Section 21, Agriculture, Fisheries and Food The next item is the debate on section 21, Ministry of Agriculture, Fisheries and Food. For the section presentation, I have the floor on behalf of the Government, the Minister for Agriculture, Fisheries and Food.
ParlaMint-ES_2021-11-23-CD211123.u198	Good afternoon, Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, I appear before the House to set out the main lines of the draft State General Budgets for 2022 in the appropriations entered in Section 21, which corresponds to the Ministry of Agriculture, Fisheries and Food. The content of Section 21, as it could not be less, is situated in the same dynamic as the rest of this draft law on the general budgets of the State, that is, the dynamism and transformation of the Spanish economy and its recovery towards new objectives for all citizens, territories and sectors, which we obviously want to be fair, and it is essential that the European funds, both the common agricultural policy and the Plan for Recovery, Transformation and Resilience, should also be referred to them - because they are for the agricultural world - as well as the Plan for Recovery, Transformation and Resilience. The budget of the Ministry of Agriculture exceeds last year's budget by more than 5%. Specifically, the consolidated budget amounts to EUR 8934 million, which is EUR 438 million more than in the previous period, and last year also grew significantly. This is undoubtedly the largest budget in the history of the Ministry of Agriculture, Fisheries and Food. In addition, EUR 503 million from the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism is included, all in an approach that is to achieve in these budgets a promotion of innovation and the consolidation of the competitiveness and profitability of the agricultural, livestock and fishing sectors, as well as of the agri-food industry. In any case, I would say that strategically these budgets tend to support the transition to more sustainable food systems, in that transition necessary to also achieve a more just and fairer functioning of the food chain. The primary sector is, ladies and gentlemen, a strategic part of our economy; it demonstrated it during the pandemic and is demonstrating it in this recovery playing a central role, as I say, in the whole of our economy, and it has to do it in just conditions. That is why the bill amending the food chain law that this House will pass next week is key for the whole of our country. There are two elements that I would like to emphasise in the budget of the Ministry of Agriculture, Fisheries and Food as regards, logically, European funds. Firstly, the common agricultural policy, the management of Community funds, because they are essential in order to maintain the income of our farmers and to a good number of the objectives to which I shall refer later. In this budget for the year 2022, these funds are increased by 3.6% compared to the figures for the year 2021. They are exactly EUR 7426 million from the European Agricultural Guarantee Fund and the European Rural Development Fund. The first pillar, that of the Feaga, reaches 5682 million euros, and the EAFRD an expenditure of 1744 million euros. With regard to the first pillar, direct aid, farmers' income support has a budget of EUR 2805 million, a very significant amount; climate and environmental benefit payments of EUR 1386 million; production-related aid for sectors in difficulty with sensitive socio-economic problems has EUR 612 million, of which 76% is dedicated to our livestock, especially to extensive livestock and the dairy sector. The payment supplement to young farmers in the amount of EUR 54.4 million, as well as the sectoral and market aid and measures programmes in the amount of EUR 555 million, which support sectors such as wine, fruit, vegetables, the beekeeping programme or school fruit and milk programmes, is also included. Finally, I would like to mention in this section what concerns the support programme for the Canary Islands, the Poseican, which has €268.4 million of support, which is tremendously important, in addition to the 18 million, which I will mention later, financed by the Ministry of Agriculture, Fisheries and Food. With regard to measures to support rural development, these are financed by European funds in the amount - as I said earlier - of EUR 1744 million, but co-financed by the General Administration of the State and the Autonomous Communities. In particular, as far as the State is concerned, co-financing will amount to EUR 158.6 million, an increase of 50 % over the year 2021. In this way, we want to strengthen the State's support for rural development measures as part of the new programming 21-27 of the common agricultural policy. These rural development measures are as important as investments in farms and agri-food industries, installation of young farmers, agri-environmental measures and organic farming, aid to areas with natural constraints or support for local initiative in rural areas through Leader groups. With regard to the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan, we will count on 503.6 million for 2022, an amount of which we will devote 303 million to the modernization of irrigation, which is a fundamental element for the environmental transformation of the sector and economic recovery. This is a fundamental chapter in the work of the ministry because, of course, it is one of the most significant from the environmental point of view and from the point of view of the productivity of our farms. The improvement in the competitiveness of agriculture and livestock will count on EUR 118.3 million to modernize farms with precision agriculture, another EUR 10 million will be allocated to computer developments to facilitate the best application for users of the common agricultural policy and, finally, the strategy of digitisation of the agri-food sector and the rural environment will have a budget of EUR 14.8 million. As far as agriculture and food is concerned, the General Secretariat will have EUR 503.5 million, 12.2% more than the previous year, and I would like to state the main priorities telegraphically. The first of these, production quality and agricultural markets, to increase the competitiveness and sustainability of agricultural and livestock holdings, EUR 45.9 million. The modernisation of machinery and agricultural technology will count on EUR 6.6 million, is the commitment to the Renovation Plan of the agricultural machinery, in addition to what we have stated in relation to Saeca for the credits, for the guarantees in relation to the loans for the acquisition of new machinery. Likewise, the allocation of the promotion to beekeeping, with 4.7 million euros, and the enhancement of the sustainability and competitiveness of livestock biodiversity, especially as regards native breeds of Spain, 4.9 million euros; the improvement of the health of agricultural production, 20 million for animal health and 15 million for plant health, to improve the infrastructures and buildings of the national reference laboratories and, also, to improve the resilience of the agri-food system to the impact of emerging pests and diseases - a matter of maximum topicality within the framework of the concept of a single health - also the support for external trade and control at the agricultural and livestock borders,500,000 euros more, for international control of goods - we are currently in a net export balance of 57,400 million euros, with a trade balance of more than 20 billion euros, a record number, without a doubt, of our economy and of our agri-food sector -. I would also like to mention the strengthening of material and human resources of the Food Information and Control Agency, which is essential for the implementation of all the reforms that we have introduced in this reform that the House will consider next week and that we are carrying out last year of the food chain law. As far as food promotion is concerned, EUR 14.9 million more. In these chapters on rural development, innovation and agri-food training, EUR 158.6 million, as mentioned above, to co-finance rural development programmes, as well as new, very interesting programmes, for example, the Culture Programme for the exchange of young people and the experiences of young farmers, farmers and livestock within Spain, EUR 1 million and also for rural women with a budget of EUR 1.5 million. On the subject of digital transformation for the promotion of training and counselling, specifically, a figure close to EUR 5 million. As I indicated above, in the Ministry's own funds budget, 18 million euros in support of the Posei for the Canary Islands. I would not like to conclude this short telegraphic statement without mentioning the nearly EUR 7 million that we are going to use in order to multiply them up to EUR 90 million in terms of the European Fund for Food Aid for the Most Disadvantaged, with EU funding. In the fisheries chapter, the General Secretariat for Fisheries will have €72.8 million. It is 11.2% more than in 2021 and, with the sum of the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan, it delivers a total of EUR 130.3 million. All this in two budgetary programmes: the protection of fisheries resources and sustainable development, and the improvement of fisheries structures and markets, in particular to support producer organisations and promote business management, improved planning, competitiveness and profitability, and also to strengthen the competitiveness of the aquaculture sector through technological development and innovation. Similarly, we will develop the social pillar of the fisheries sector, advancing towards effective gender equality in this sector, and promoting participatory local development, through the Spanish Network of Fishing Groups. This entire budget focuses on the management of the European Maritime and Fisheries Fund, EUR 8 million, or the consolidation of the Spanish leadership in the fight against undeclared and unregulated illegal fishing, to which will be dedicated EUR 10,5 million for fisheries control and inspection activities. The 57.5 million that we will implement in the fisheries sector under the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan will focus on the modernisation of marine reserves of fisheries interest, fisheries research - which is essential for the sustainability of the activity, through collaboration with the main scientific institutes - and fisheries training actions, as well as technological development and innovation in the fisheries and aquaculture sector for the boost of blue growth in Spain, and finally on aid for fisheries digitisation and for the means of surveillance of the fishing fleet. Finally, I refer to one of the most beloved chapters in the activity of this ministry, a chapter I would say substantial, which refers to the policy of agricultural insurance. It originated in 1978 and is a fundamental pillar of agricultural policy; it is a reference model throughout the European Union, of which we can be very proud, and which allows an effective tool for maintaining incomes and the economic viability of farms, in the face of the occurrence of accidents that escape the control of farmers and farmers, such as those caused by adverse natural phenomena. We are going to devote EUR 256.7 million to all this, and with regard to the subsidy for agricultural insurance premiums. Last year we recovered 23 million euros; this year we added 3 more in order to increase, also with the support of the autonomous communities, those insurance premiums that are so important to our farmers and winners. Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, I conclude. These are budgets for the recovery in the agricultural, livestock, fishing and in general for the whole agri-food sector, which are clearly aimed at relaunching our economy, strengthening the agri-food sector and modernizing its productive fabric, in line with the new challenges and transformations posed by this decade towards the sustainability of agri-food production, as well as promoting equal opportunities and territorial cohesion in Spain. We will make every effort to make it an effective instrument for a more modern, efficient and fairer primary sector - agriculture, fisheries, food. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]
